# Rust Port Status

This document tracks Rust implementation slices and the original C source units they are intended to mirror.

## Factoring

Rust files:

- `src/clauses/factor.rs`

C source references:

- `eprover/CLAUSES/ccl_factor.c`
- `eprover/CLAUSES/ccl_factor.h`
- `eprover/CONTROL/cco_factoring.c`
- `eprover/CONTROL/cco_factoring.h`

Implemented:

- First-order ordered factor candidate enumeration matching `ClausePosFirstOrderedFactorLiterals` / `ClausePosNextOrderedFactorLiterals`.
- First-order `ComputeOrderedFactor`, including second-literal side retry, directed unification, post-unifier maximality recheck, normalized copy excluding the second literal, and resolved/duplicate cleanup.
- First-order `ComputeAllOrderedFactors`, including Horn and `CPNoGeneration` gates, clause-set insertion, parent proof-depth/proof-size/TPTP/SOS metadata propagation, `DCOrderedFactor` derivation entries, and opt-in represented `inf_factor` proof-documentation output.
- First-order equality factor candidate enumeration matching `ClausePosFirstEqualityFactorSides` / `ClausePosNextEqualityFactorSides`.
- First-order MGU subset of `ComputeEqualityFactor` and `ComputeAllEqualityFactors`, including higher-order problem mode when candidate terms have no lambda/DB-variable/phony-application surface and accepted substitutions have no higher-order binding, plus the C free-variable/equational guard, `TOGreater` side check, post-unifier maximality recheck, generated negative condition, DB-lambda beta/eta normalization of generated equality-factor literal lists before cleanup, Horn and `CPNoGeneration` gates, clause-set insertion, parent metadata propagation, `DCEqFactor` derivation entries, and opt-in represented `inf_efactor` proof-documentation output.

Pending:

- Full higher-order CSU enumeration, higher-order derivation-flag propagation, and multi-CSU equality-factor stack order.
- Reusable fresh-variable-bank behavior may need a performance pass once stable clause/literal ownership is ported.

Change-later notes:

- C equality factoring pushes CSU results to a stack and the wrapper later pops them, reversing multi-CSU insertion order. Rust currently has only the one-result first-order subset; preserve or intentionally change the reversal only after HO trace tests exist.
- C maps `SubstHasHOBinding` into higher-order `DCEqFactor` derivation metadata for each accepted substitution. Rust keeps first-order-subset results as ordinary `DCEqFactor` and reports a CSU diagnostic for arrow-typed bindings until full HO derivation handling is represented.

## Equality Resolution

Rust files:

- `src/clauses/eqnresolution.rs`

C source references:

- `eprover/CLAUSES/ccl_eqnresolution.c`
- `eprover/CLAUSES/ccl_eqnresolution.h`

Implemented:

- First-order `ComputeEqRes` and `ComputeAllEqnResolvents`, including the higher-order problem-mode first-order subset when the selected disequality has no lambda/DB-variable/phony-application surface and the accepted substitution has no higher-order binding.
- C-shaped non-selected literal substitution normalization, optimized copy excluding the resolved literal, DB-lambda beta/eta normalization of copied resolvents before cleanup, false-literal removal, duplicate removal, maximal negative-literal iteration, caller-owned clause-set insertion, `DCEqRes` derivation entries, destructive equality-resolution normalization with `DCDesEqRes`, and represented proof-documentation helpers for creation and destructive replacement.

Pending:

- Full higher-order CSU enumeration, higher-order derivation-flag propagation, and multi-CSU stack order.

Change-later notes:

- C `ComputeEqRes` switches between returning one clause and filling a result stack based on whether `res_cls` is NULL. Rust keeps separate helpers so the API does not depend on a null stack argument.
- C pushes higher-order CSU resolvents to `res_cls` and `ComputeAllEqnResolvents` pops that stack before insertion, reversing enumeration order. The current first-order subset has at most one result per literal; revisit the order only with HO trace tests.
- C maps `SubstHasHOBinding` into higher-order `DCEqRes` derivation metadata. Rust keeps higher-order-problem first-order subset results as ordinary `DCEqRes` and reports a CSU diagnostic for arrow-typed bindings until full HO derivation handling is represented.

## Paramodulation

- `src/clauses/paramodulation.rs`
- `src/clauses/overlap_index.rs`

C source references:

- `eprover/CLAUSES/ccl_paramod.h`
- `eprover/CONTROL/cco_paramodulation.c`
- `eprover/CONTROL/cco_paramodulation.h`

Implemented:

- First-order `ComputeOverlap`, including MGU construction, ordered source-side rejection, substitution normalization before term-bank insertion, and first-order `TBTermPosReplace` rebuilding.
- First-order `EqnOrderedParamod`, including target-side ordering rejection, positive trivial-paramodulant elimination, negative trivial-paramodulant retention for resolved-literal cleanup, and `EPIsPMIntoLit` marking.
- First-order low-level `ClauseOrderedParamod` for explicit clause positions, including strict maximality rechecks after substitution, normalized copies excluding source/target literals, `EPFromClauseLit` and `EPIsPMIntoLit` flag flow, DB-lambda beta/eta normalization of the generated literal list before cleanup, resolved-literal cleanup, and duplicate cleanup.
- First-order paramodulation source/target/pair candidate enumeration matching `ClausePosFirst/NextParamodFromSide`, `ClausePosFirst/NextParamodInto`, and `ClausePosFirst/NextParamodPair` cursor order for the plain and ordinary simultaneous paths, including selected-source rejection, positive-root `no_top` skipping, target-variable skipping, non-equational predicate-root skipping for free-variable sources, and `TPPotentialParamod` marking for simultaneous targets.
- Non-equational predicate paramodulation into maximal negative predicate literals, including the C cursor behavior that still enumerates the `$true` side of non-oriented positive predicate literals and relies on unification failure to discard useless `$true`-source overlaps.
- Plain first-order unindexed `ComputeClauseClauseParamodulants` and `ComputeAllParamodulants`, plus the first-order-shaped higher-order KBO6 selected-overlap subset, including `CPNoGeneration` gates, first/reverse direction order with reverse `no_top`, generated-clause insertion into a caller store, proof-depth/proof-size/TPTP/SOS metadata propagation, `DCParamod` derivation entries on generated clauses, opt-in represented `inf_paramod` proof-documentation output, and copying of unrelated higher-order applied-variable literals when the selected overlap terms stay first-order-shaped.
- Simultaneous and super-simultaneous first-order unindexed `ComputeClauseClauseParamodulants` and `ComputeAllParamodulants`, including the `TPPotentialParamod` duplicate-suppression flag, ordinary whole-target-clause occurrence replacement through `EqnListCopyRepl`, super-simultaneous instantiated-target copying before replacement, positive strict-maximal and negative maximal target rechecks, copied-literal tautology rejection, DB-lambda beta/eta normalization before resolved/duplicate cleanup, `DCSimParamod` derivation entries, and opt-in represented `inf_sim_paramod` proof-documentation output.
- First-order indexed `ComputeAllParamodulantsIndexed` over caller-provided overlap indexes, plus the first-order-shaped higher-order KBO6 selected-overlap subset, including the C two-phase source-into-indexed-targets and indexed-sources-into-selected-targets traversal, negative-predicate/ordinary-into split, term-level MGU/order filtering of fingerprint false positives before compact-position construction, positive top-level reverse skip, compact-position unpacking, parent-alias metadata propagation, plain/simultaneous/super-simultaneous mode dispatch, indexed simultaneous duplicate suppression per target clause term, instantiated-target replacement for super-simultaneous construction, generated-list DB-lambda beta/eta normalization through the shared constructors, `DCParamod`/`DCSimParamod` derivation entries, and opt-in represented paramodulation proof-documentation output.
- Proof-control integration for selected-clause generation through the plain, simultaneous, and super-simultaneous unindexed paths, including unconditional, oriented-source, order-decreasing, and size-decreasing simultaneous mode selection, negative-unit strategy gating, `paramod_count` updates, and opt-in paramodulation creation docs.
- Proof-control selected-clause generation through caller-owned global paramodulation indexes, including C's indexed-when-available branch shape, fallback to the unindexed processed-set scan when PM indexes are absent, and opt-in paramodulation creation docs for the caller-owned indexed path.
- `ProcessClause` and `Saturate` integration through caller-owned global paramodulation indexes, preserving C's processed-survivor global-index insertion before watchlist simplification and selected-clause generation, carrying that indexed branch through the outer saturation loop, and deleting caller-owned global-index entries before reset, move-to-tmp, or backward-simplification movement removes processed clauses.
- Supported first-order executable proof search now builds configured caller-owned global indices after `ProofStateInit`, using a cloned initialized signature, and drives presaturation, `ProofStateResetProcessed`, and main saturation through the indexed path.

Pending:

- Full state-owned indexed paramodulation once a proof-session owner can hold global indices together with the mutable term bank and live signature without the cloned-signature executable bridge.
- Full higher-order unification constraints, lambda/DB/phony-application surfaces, non-KBO6 higher-order ordering backends, lambda normalization, and higher-order proof-documentation effects.
- The optional C `check_paramod_ordering_constraint` path remains disabled in C and unported in Rust.

Change-later notes:

- The C implementation encodes inference provenance through mutable literal flags (`EPIsPMIntoLit`, `EPFromClauseLit`) that selection heuristics later inspect. Rust currently preserves those flags for compatibility; a typed generated-literal metadata layer may be clearer after proof reconstruction is stable.
- Simultaneous paramodulation uses `TPPotentialParamod` as a mutable marker on shared terms to suppress duplicate rewrites after the first successful all-occurrences replacement. Rust preserves that C-shaped state; a future per-inference marker table would be less fragile once compatibility and benchmark baselines are fixed.
- Fresh-variable-bank seeding is duplicated with factoring for now. It should become a shared helper once clause ownership and inference wrapper APIs settle.
- C `ComputeAllParamodulantsIndexed` does not repeat the unindexed wrapper's explicit `CPNoGeneration` parent/candidate gate. Rust mirrors that indexed shape for compatibility; verify whether globally indexed `CPNoGeneration` clauses can occur before deciding whether this is a C bug worth cleaning up later.
- Rust can test and drive the indexed wrapper with caller-owned indexes over a cloned signature, including through selected-clause generation, `ProcessClause`, and `Saturate`; the supported executable path uses that bridge for first-order clause-list proof search, while full proof-control still needs a proof-session owner that can hold global indexes tied to the live term-bank signature while allowing generated clauses to mutate the term bank.
- C global indexes use live clause pointer identity, so Rust must delete entries while a clause is still in its processed set; deleting after extraction would not match the stored pointer-shaped key. Keep this C-compatible lifecycle until processed clauses have stable Rust handles.
- C indexed order-decreasing simultaneous mode decides `sim_paramod_q` while the unifier's bindings are active; Rust mirrors that for indexed mode selection, but the extra trial MGU should be benchmarked once proof-control can drive the indexed path.
- C's non-equational predicate paramodulation keeps using side-level cursors, so `p(a) = $true` contributes both `p(a)` and `$true` as source sides even though only the predicate side can usefully overlap a negative atom. Rust now mirrors this for compatibility; after parity, an explicit predicate-resolution path could skip the dead `$true` source candidate while preserving candidate order where it matters.
- C dispatches ordinary paramodulation after `ComputeHOInferences` even in higher-order problem mode, relying on lower LFHO unification and ordering code to handle any higher-order surfaces. Rust currently uses explicit first-order-shaped KBO6 guards before reusing the first-order paramodulation path; revisit this compatibility boundary after full LFHO ordering and CSU enumeration are ported.

## Disequality Decomposition

Rust files:

- `src/clauses/diseq_decomp.rs`

C source references:

- `eprover/CLAUSES/ccl_diseq_decomp.c`
- `eprover/CLAUSES/ccl_diseq_decomp.h`
- `eprover/CONTROL/cco_diseq_decomp.c`
- `eprover/CONTROL/cco_diseq_decomp.h`

Implemented:

- `ClauseDisEqDecomposition`, including compact-position selection, residual literal copy, reversed argument-pair disequality generation, resolved/duplicate cleanup, and `DCDisEqDecompose` derivation metadata.
- `ComputeDisEqDecompositions`, including the literal-count threshold, negative equational top-symbol/arity gates, max-arity gate, clause-set insertion, and generated count.

Proof-control integration:

- `generate_new_clauses` now calls the Rust higher-order ArgCong, NegExt, PosExt, inverse-recognition, Leibniz-elimination, PrimitiveEnumeration, and choice-instantiation helpers before first-order generators and the disequality-decomposition helper when C's literal-count and max-arity gates allow it. Caller-owned HO extension indexes can drive indexed ExtSup plus ExtEqRes/ExtEqFact for the grouped extensional-rule branch. Plain, simultaneous, and super-simultaneous first-order unindexed paramodulation, plus first-order-shaped higher-order KBO6 selected-overlap paramodulation that can copy unrelated applied-variable literals, are wired after disequality decomposition with opt-in creation docs; explicit caller-owned global-index variants can drive indexed paramodulation from selected-clause generation, `ProcessClause`, and `Saturate`, and supported first-order executable proof search uses configured caller-owned global indexes; remaining full state-owned indexed generation branches remain pending.

Change-later notes:

- C builds disequality-decomposition argument pairs by pushing each generated negative literal at the front, so the final generated clause lists decomposed arguments in reverse source-argument order. Rust preserves that order for proof and output compatibility; a later cleaned API should separate user-facing clause order from the C construction shortcut only after reference traces allow it.
- The literal-count and max-arity gates are spread across the C control wrapper and helper-level assertions. Rust keeps those gates at the staged proof-control boundary for now; once proof-control owns all generation settings, expose them as typed configuration instead of relying on callers to reproduce C preconditions.
- Inverse-recognition proof metadata is attached inside C `ClauseRecognizeInjectivity`, while `InferInjectiveDefinition` only inserts the returned clause and the C proof-documentation call is still a TODO. Rust mirrors that placement and absence; revisit the API boundary when proof-document output is owned separately from clause construction.
- PrimitiveEnumeration keeps C's per-clause applied-variable head de-duplication, unconditional true/false substitutions, pointer-identity ordering for distinct Eq-mode return types, and post-`DCPrimEnum` Boolean simplification. Revisit these only after higher-order trace comparison shows which details are proof-search policy and which are construction artifacts.

- Parser, lambda-lift generalization reuse, formula SInE/relevance call sites, executable formula-owner app-encode integration for FOOL presentation and clause-backed wrapped formulas, formula proof-document integration, and stable formula handles beyond the represented proof-state owner sets.

Change-later notes:

- C represents `FormulaSet` as an intrusive doubly linked list by mutating `set`, `pred`, and `succ` fields inside each `WFormula`. Rust currently keeps formulas by value in an owned vector with stable entry ids; revisit arena/stable-handle ownership and O(1) front extraction when executable formula archives need C-like pointer identity or list performance.
- C `WFormulaGetId` combines a process-global generated-id counter with the mutable `FormulasKeepInputNames` global. Rust exposes input-name preservation as an explicit argument for the staged helper; decide later whether executable compatibility needs a global policy shim or whether call sites can stay explicit.
- C `WFormulaPrint` dispatches through the process-global `OutputFormat`, and the LOP case emits a warning before intentionally falling through to TPTP output. Rust uses an explicit `FormulaPrintFormat` for staged deterministic rendering; executable integration must decide where to reproduce the warning and global dispatch.
- C `WFormClauseToClause` allocates a fresh `Clause` from the formula payload, copies wrapper properties and source info, but does not preserve the wrapper id as the printed clause id. Rust mirrors that staged behavior; decide during proof-state integration whether clause/formula conversion should preserve a stable wrapper handle separately from generated clause ids.
- C `WFormulaOfClause` closes the encoded clause into a fresh formula wrapper but does not copy clause properties or source info. Rust mirrors that metadata drop; a later proof-document owner should decide whether compatibility output needs separate parent metadata rather than overloading the new wrapper.
- C `WFormulaTSTPPrintFlex` prints `as_formula=false` clause wrappers through `ClauseTSTPCorePrint`; typed first-order wrappers get a `tcf(...)` header but still use the raw clause core instead of `ClauseTSTPPrint`'s universally closed typed clause body. Rust mirrors the split; revisit only with reference output coverage because changing it affects proof/document text.
- C `FormulaSetSimplify` interleaves formula mutation, optional term-bank GC checks, `DocFormulaModificationDefault`, and `DCFofSimplify` derivation pushes. Rust now stages mutation/counting, mirrors the optional C-thresholded GC checks for callers that request them, and exposes `DCFofSimplify` opcodes plus recovered-term counts in result metadata; connect the formula-owned derivation stack when formula proof-document ownership is ported.
- C's `WFormulaConjectureNegate` comment says it sets `WPInitialConjecture`, but this checkout has no `WPInitialConjecture` symbol and the implementation only negates the formula, changes the role to `CPTypeNegConjecture`, documents the modification, and pushes `DCNegateConjecture`. Rust follows the executable code path and treats the comment as stale until a different upstream source proves otherwise.
- C question/conjecture preprocessing interleaves `WFormulaAnnotateQuestion`, `WFormulaConjectureNegate`, formula derivation pushes, proof-document output, and optional answer-skolem association through `question_assoc`. Rust now stages the formula mutation and returns `DCAnnoQuestion`/`DCNegateConjecture` opcodes, but it does not yet store formula-owned derivations, proof-document side effects, or `question_assoc`.
- C `WFormulaSetUnrollFOOL` records `DCEqToEq` for the Boolean-equality replacement phase but returns/counts only formulas changed by the later `do_fool_unroll` mapper; literal expansion can still mutate a formula without `DCFoolUnroll`. Rust now exposes separate staged counters/opcodes, but formula-owned derivation/proof-document side effects remain deferred.
- C `TFormulaSetIntroduceDefs` reuses one `NumXTree` cell for polarity, virtual definition id, real blocking definition id, and archived neutral-definition pointer while interleaving formula creation docs, `DCIntroDef`/`DCFofQuote`/`DCSplitEquiv`, and later `DCApplyDef` derivations. Rust now uses explicit staged definition entries and preserves archive/active insertion order plus opcode metadata, but formula-owned derivation stacks, proof-document output, and stable formula-parent handles remain deferred.
- C `TFormulaSetDelTermpProp` silently skips wrappers with a null `tformula` while clearing the requested term flags recursively with `DEREF_NEVER`. Rust exposes this as a no-op for staged default wrappers; revisit only if executable formula-set insertion policy must assert on formula-less wrappers.
- C `TFormulaSetNamedToDBLambdas` calls `NamedToDB` over every higher-order formula even though the lower-level conversion expects named-lambda cells when it sees lambda syntax. Rust guards DB-lambda-only formulas as no-ops before calling the named-lambda converter, preserving the intended set phase while avoiding a staged-owner panic before the later DB-lambda normalization phase.
- C `FormulaSetArchive` moves originals to the archive and puts flat copies back into the source set, with `DCFofQuote` pushed only on the replacement copy. Rust preserves the movement/replacement order and exposes quote-source metadata, but the actual formula-owned derivation stack remains deferred.
- C `FormulaSetDocInital` uses the misspelled helper name, level-gates at `OutputLevel >= 2`, and assigns fresh formula proof-document ids while printing initial formula docs. Rust now mirrors the level gate, insertion order, and id assignment through `ProofDocSession` and represented formula views; executable formula-archive call sites still need to wire this into parsing/preprocessing.
- C `WFormulaCNF2` mutates the wrapper formula and pushes formula-level derivation entries during each changed CNF phase. Rust now exposes those phase opcodes in the staged result but does not yet store a formula-owned derivation stack; add that stack when formula archives/proof documents become real owners.
- C `TFormulaSetLiftItes` uses `map_formula`, `do_ite_unroll`, `TermFindIteSubterm`, and term-position replacement helpers over shared term-bank cells. Rust now mirrors formula-position ITE expansion, literal-side term-position ITE expansion, left-before-right literal scanning, higher-order gating, and `DCLiftIte` metadata, but app-flattening behavior, lambda-bound subterm cases, and deeper formula-owner interactions should still be audited against C reference traces before deciding whether those C implementation details should be preserved or cleaned up.
- C `TFormulaSetLiftLets` interleaves quantified-variable renaming, f-code-keyed local-definition replacement, fresh symbol allocation over captured and formal variables, Boolean-definition equivalence construction, root `unencode_eqns`, stack-reversed insertion of generated definitions, and `DCIntroDef`/`DCApplyDef` provenance updates. Rust now mirrors that behavior in the staged formula-set owner, but reference traces should still cover nested lets, captured variable order, app flattening, and proof-document side effects before deciding whether C's f-code-keyed replacement map is a compatibility requirement or just an implementation shortcut.
- C `TFormulaSetLiftLambdas` walks the active formula set, mutates formulas whose term payloads contain lambdas, pushes `DCIntroDef` for each generated definition, then inserts the unique generated definitions into the same active set after traversal. Rust now stages that owner behavior while seeding `VarBank` counts before fresh variable generation; C omits that seed in the standalone set pass even though `ClauseSetLiftLambdas` performs it, so keep exact seeding parity as a reference-trace question if variable numbering becomes observable.
- C `TFormulaSetUnfoldDefSymbols` recognizes only `CPIsLambdaDef` wrappers, strips leading universal quantifiers, accepts equation and predicate-equivalence definition shapes, uses a symbol f-code map, freshens lambda binders before applying both sides, temporarily marks definition arguments as special variables, rejects self-recursive right sides and definitions with remaining free variables, intersimplifies generated definitions, recursively rewrites formulas with a 500-step counter, inserts generated flat-copy definitions into the archive, and then extracts the recognized original definitions into the archive. Rust now mirrors those staged owner effects, but C's post-recursion step decrement, special-variable flag mutation, duplicate f-code overwrite behavior, `unfold_only_forms` predicate gate, and proof-document side effects should stay visible until reference traces decide what must remain compatibility behavior.
- C `ClauseSetLiftLambdas` decodes formula-shaped clause terms, calls `LiftLambdas`, pushes `DCLiftLambdas` with each generated definition, archives the generated definition formula, optionally FOOL-unrolls and simplifies a flat copy, clausifies that copy back into the same clause set, and does not add those definition clauses to `FormulaSetCNF2`'s returned clause count. Rust now stages those observable post-CNF side effects, but C's PDTree-based `find_generalization` can reuse a more general earlier lifting while the staged Rust passes currently create fresh definitions for lifted lambda occurrences; keep that optimization visible for a later compatibility/performance pass.
- C `FormulaSetCNF2` first runs the higher-order named-to-DB, ITE lifting, LET lifting, definition-symbol unfolding, and optional lambda-to-forall normalization phases before FOOL unrolling, simplification, definition introduction, archive movement, clausification, optional post-CNF clause lambda lifting, and C-thresholded GC. Rust now stages named-to-DB, ITE lifting, LET lifting, definition-symbol unfolding, lambda-to-forall normalization, and post-CNF clause lambda lifting in those positions, preserves the archive order, exposes quote sources, and reports CNF-path GC collections/recovered terms, but the actual formula derivation stack and proof-document side effects remain deferred.
- C `FormulaSetPrettyPrintTSTP` discovers the signature through the first formula's term bank and prints all known type declarations when the set is not fully untyped. Rust takes the bank explicitly while preserving declaration-before-formula ordering; decide during executable integration whether first-entry signature coupling and declaration leakage from unrelated symbols must be emulated.
- C `WFormulaGCMarkCells` ultimately asserts on null term-bank/formula inputs through `TBGCMarkTerm`, even though the wrapper comment describes non-term formulas as a no-op. Rust treats a staged `WrappedFormula` without a formula term as a no-op during GC marking so default allocation remains useful before insertion policy is finalized; revisit if C-compatible assertion behavior becomes observable.
- C `WFormulaMarkPolarity` has the same assumed non-null formula shape as GC marking. Rust currently treats a staged wrapper without a formula term as a no-op during set-level polarity marking; revisit alongside insertion policy and exact assertion compatibility.
- C `WFormulaGetLambdaDefinedSym` accepts an `equiv(eqn(lhs, rhs), ...)` lambda-definition shape without checking that `rhs` is `$true`, but `FormulaSetDefinitionStatistics` only counts that predicate-definition shape when `rhs` is literally the shared `$true` term. Rust mirrors both shapes in their respective helper paths; keep this mismatch visible when connecting formula statistics to proof-state feature extraction.
- C `FormulaSetDefinitionStatistics` writes `int` counters through caller pointers. Rust returns a typed statistics struct and saturates the staged `i32` counters; decide during proof-state feature integration whether executable compatibility needs exact C-width behavior or wider Rust feature counters.

Proof-found and stopped/saturation list output now expands represented clause ancestors, prints each represented parent before children that cite it, and uses display-only sequential ids with represented clause-parent and rewrite-demodulator parent references remapped for output.

Pending:

- Stable clause/formula parent handles instead of compact `ident`/source references.
- Stable proof-state ownership/traversal for AC axiom parent references.
- Formula-owned derivation stacks, optional-parent replacement, full proof-object extraction, exact C sibling ordering and renumbering, full PCL/TSTP derived-step printing over ordered clause/formula derivations, formula-aware DOT labels, and formula-aware proof-object analysis.

Change-later notes:

- C stores raw `Clause_p` and `WFormula_p` parent pointers in derivation stacks. Rust stores compact clause identifiers paired with source tags plus compact formula identifiers for current proof-depth, inference-count, and derivation-rendering behavior, but proof reconstruction should move to stable clause/formula handles before duplicate identifiers, archived copies, and requeued clauses become harder to distinguish.
- C `WFormulaGetId` can preserve source formula names through the mutable `FormulasKeepInputNames` global, while Rust represented formula derivation refs currently render only generated `c_0_...`/`i_0_...` ids. Revisit named formula ids when real `WFormula` ownership and formula source-name policy are ported.
- C `ClausePushACResDerivation` records only `PStackGetSP(sig->ac_axioms)` rather than the AC axiom parents themselves. Rust now records compact AC axiom refs on `Signature` and expands them for context-aware derivation rendering, but the derivation push still takes the C count explicitly; prefer stable proof-state parent handles once full proof-object traversal owns clauses.
- C's final proof-object list display order is tied to its pointer-backed extraction/traversal and display-id assignment. Rust now enforces the compatibility requirement that displayed parents precede children, but exact C sibling ordering and formula/clause interleaving should be revisited with reference proof-output traces before replacing the temporary topological order with a more polished renderer.
- C proof-documentation pushes are interleaved with inference construction, simplification, archive movement, and global output. Rust now exposes several represented opt-in proof-documentation wrappers, but executable-wide output/session ownership remains separate; the eventual proof object layer should keep metadata creation separate from rendering so compatibility shims can reproduce C streams without making them the core API.

Pending:

- Byte-for-byte `CSSCPA_filter` comparison against a built C executable remains pending for the full help stream and platform-specific system-error suffixes.
- Exact scanner-format provenance for historical `.csscpa` files that use old `input_clause(...)` syntax even though `CSSCPA_filter.c` sets `TSTPFormat` before invoking `CSSCPALoop`.

Change-later notes:

- C keeps `terms` and `tmp_terms` as separate term banks over one shared mutable signature. Rust currently allocates a fresh tautology work bank from the live signature for each processed clause; revisit persistent scratch-bank reuse after shared signature ownership is available.
- C stores raw subsumed-clause pointers before later deletion. Rust records clause identifiers and the owning CSSCPA bucket because `ClauseSet` owns clauses by value; stable clause handles would make the deletion path closer to C and may matter if CSSCPA becomes performance-critical.
- C embeds `GlobalOut` printing and unconditional flushing inside `CSSCPAProcessClause`, including an extra flush from `print_csscpa_state` before the process-clause trailing flush. Rust keeps process-global output out of the core API by returning trace text plus flush offsets; future streaming or cleaned CSSCPA APIs should preserve these boundaries only in drop-in compatibility mode.
- C silently consumes unsupported `output_level` positive integers without changing the current output gate, treats negative CLI output levels as nonzero for `if(OutputLevel)` traces but below `OUTPRINT(1)`, and prints `state:` requests regardless of the gate. Rust preserves those numeric gate quirks; a later cleaned CLI could make unsupported levels and state-output policy explicit after compatibility runs are stable.
- C's filter uses process-global `GlobalOut`, while Rust routes output through explicit writers or a local output file. Keep the explicit API unless byte-compatible `OpenGlobalOut` side effects become observable.
- C scanner streams are lazy `FILE*` readers. Rust's filter currently reads stdin and files into scanner-owned memory before tokenization; revisit this if CSSCPA workloads expose large-input memory or latency differences.

Keep this as an adapter boundary so later platform event loops can target either exact `select` behavior or a cleaner poll abstraction without changing session state semantics.

- `EPCtrlGetResult` has table entries for `Failure` and `GaveUp`, but the scanner only recognizes theorem, contradictory axioms, unsatisfiable, satisfiable, and counter-satisfiable lines; EOF without one of those statuses becomes `PRFailure`. Rust preserves that parser surface for now.
- `ECtrlCreateGeneric` builds one shell command by string concatenation without quoting `prover`, options, or file names. Rust preserves exact command construction in the helper but uses structured process arguments for safe spawning; reserve shell concatenation for compatibility mode if scheduler reference tests require it.
- `EPCtrlSetGetResult` ignores `select` errors, scans integer descriptors from `0..=maxfd`, and asserts on impossible result states. Rust keeps the deterministic polling behavior explicit; the later event-loop adapter should decide which of these artifacts are compatibility requirements.
- Rust's portable process-set timeout poll uses per-child reader threads and channels rather than blocking the main thread in OS `select`, because Windows child stdout handles are not `select`-compatible sockets. Keep exact descriptor readiness as a compatibility adapter concern if byte-level scheduler tests require it.

Change-later notes:

- `BatchSpecParse` is explicitly described as wonky in C: comments and newlines are structurally meaningful in the spec format, but the implementation ignores them and relies on scanner token flow. Rust mirrors the token flow for compatibility; later, a strict grammar should be considered only after reference corpus coverage exists.
- `e_ltb_runner` accepts `division.category.training_data`, while `BatchSpecPrint` emits `division.category.training_directory`. Rust preserves both spellings at their respective boundaries so the mismatch stays visible.
- Parsing includes prints `% Accepted ... for parsing` as a side effect before any actual axiom parsing. Keep this until runner output compatibility says whether the notice can move behind a verbosity flag.
- The problem-list loop starts only when the next token is `/` or the literal identifier `Problem|Problems`, then parses two filename-shaped token runs. This accommodates `Problems/...` paths but is not a real section grammar.
- `abstract_to_concrete` discards everything in the abstract name after the first `*`; variant processing should keep that truncation until CASC variant specs are covered by tests.
- C declares `StructFOFSpec` in `cco_sine.h`, implements allocation/parsing/distribution in `cco_sine.c`, and implements add/backtrack/get-problem helpers in `cco_batch_spec.c`. Rust keeps a single `control::sine` owner API; future cleanup can make the C split irrelevant once callers are fully ported.
- `StructFOFSpecBacktrackToSpec` subtracts formula-set distribution with the non-trimming path even when the problem was added with trimmed implication accounting. Rust preserves that by using untrimmed formula subtraction in backtracking; revisit only if full LTB/SInE traces prove this asymmetry is accidental and observable.
- C backtracking also calls `SigBacktrack` and term-bank GC through global owner state. Rust currently reports the signature f-count target from the `StructFofSpecBacktrackReport`; wiring actual signature/term-bank backtracking belongs with the future file-parser/term-bank ownership integration.
- `BatchProcessProblems` biases proportional total-time slices upward with `rest/(remaining)+1`, then caps by the per-problem limit only when that limit is nonzero. It can therefore pass negative wall-clock limits to `BatchProcessFile` after the total limit is already exceeded. Rust preserves this arithmetic for now.
- `BatchProcessProblems` constructs `dest_dir` paths by appending a literal `/` before the destination file without normalizing empty strings or trailing slashes. Rust preserves this path construction; later platform-aware path joining should be considered only after LTB output compatibility is covered.
- `e_ltb_runner` initializes shared axioms before calling `BatchProcessProblems`, then passes `MAX(0, spec->total_wtc_limit-(now-start))` so parsing/initialization consumes global time outside the per-problem loop. Rust's concrete `BatchProcessProblems` path expects an already-initialized `StructFofSpec`, and the executable wrapper now keeps that timing boundary explicit.
- `e_ltb_runner` opens `GlobalOut` after option parsing but before validating the remaining positional argument count, so `-o file` can create or truncate the output file even for a usage error. Rust now preserves that observable side effect in the wrapper; a cleaned CLI should only remove it behind a compatibility-mode decision.
- `e_ltb_runner` carries enum values and globals for obsolete or unused hooks such as `OPT_NEWSCHEDULE`, `OPT_PRINT_STATISTICS`, and `app_encode`. Rust omits those dead hooks from the executable surface for now; add them only if a reference command line proves they are observable.
- The C help text says `[Batchfile] [PATH_TO_EPROVER]`, while the fatal usage error says `<spec> [<path-to-eprover>]`. Rust keeps the fatal usage wording and uses a clearer help line; revise only if byte-for-byte CLI-help compatibility becomes a target.
- C variant modes are hard-coded to historical CASC packaging details, including relative executable paths like `./eprover-ho` and `./eprover-25`. Rust preserves those tables for parent-side variant dispatch; packaging-path behavior still needs reference tests against an actual CASC-style StarExec layout.
- C implements variant workers by returning `NULL` from `EGPCtrlCreate` in the forked child and then continuing through the same call stack into `BatchProcessFile`. Rust uses an explicit hidden child-process command instead, which is easier to audit on Windows and avoids unsafe fork-style control flow; exact fork-return semantics remain a compatibility question if byte-level traces prove the distinction observable.
- C temporarily swaps `spec->includes` with a freshly allocated concrete include stack, calls `BatchStructFOFSpecInit`, then frees and restores the original include stack. Rust keeps the original `BatchSpec` immutable and initializes from a cloned concrete include list; preserve the observable concrete `.ax` rewrite, but avoid the temporary owner mutation unless exact memory/lifetime behavior becomes relevant.
- C interactive mode separates its output stream argument from the input source but always reads jobs from stdin, uses a literal `go.\n` terminator, treats `help` and `quit` as command tokens without consuming or validating trailing input, and falls back to a 30-second per-problem limit when the batch spec omits one. Rust preserves these quirks for compatibility; a later cleaned interface should make input/output streams, command grammar, and timeout defaults explicit.
- `batch_create_runner` combines logging, SInE problem selection, temporary-file creation, type/axiom printing, process naming, and child-process creation in one helper routed through `GlobalOut`. Rust stages these as an explicit runner request for testability; later cleanup can split selection, rendering, temp-file ownership, and spawning once byte-compatible runner traces exist.
- `batch_create_runner` calls `AxFilterPrintBuf(name, 320, ax_filter)` but ignores the boolean that reports buffer overflow, so an overlong filter name can feed a truncated process name into process-control naming. Rust keeps the fixed 320-byte boundary visible and reports overflow explicitly.
- C assumes `source_files` and `dest_files` stacks have matching lengths because parsing pushes them in pairs. Rust reports an interface diagnostic for hand-built mismatched specs rather than indexing past the destination list.
- `BatchProcessProblem` leaves `handle` uninitialized if the wall-clock limit is already expired before the outer loop first executes, then tests `if(handle)`. Rust treats that path as GaveUp and still backtracks; keep this documented as an intentional cleanup of accidental C undefined behavior.
- `BatchProcessProblem` gives each child `MIN((wct_limit+1)/2, wct_limit-used)` seconds, so zero or negative per-problem limits can produce nonpositive child CPU budgets. Rust preserves the formula in the staged request path until real process-limit compatibility tests decide whether to clamp.
- `BatchProcessProblem` interleaves status construction with `GlobalOut`, optional file output, optional socket output, and interactive proof echoing. Rust stages explicit global, file-like external, and socket-like writers; a later API should separate result synthesis from output transports once byte compatibility is covered.
- The C socket path sends only the prover's proof text on success but sends a generated `% SZS status GaveUp ...` line on failure. Rust preserves that transport asymmetry for now; normalize it only after socket-client compatibility is covered.
- `BatchProcessFile` forces the scanner to TSTP format for every problem file, independent of the batch-spec parse format. Rust exposes that as a loader request field; revisit only if broader input-format compatibility proves other formats are accepted by the C runner.
- `FormulaAndClauseSetParse` puts ordinary `cnf` input into the formula set as clause-backed formulas; the clause set receives watchlist clauses. Rust's concrete LTB loader preserves that split, even though a cleaned representation would likely store ordinary parsed clauses directly as clauses.
- `StructFOFSpecParseAxioms` silently skips missing include files after printing `% Could not find ...`, and records parsed include names only after successful parsing. Rust preserves that behavior; later user-facing cleanup should decide whether missing shared axioms should ever become hard errors.
- Scanner default-directory handling expects slash-terminated directory prefixes. Rust keeps the raw C-style directory forwarding for LTB file loading; a later path-aware API should normalize only after compatibility tests cover relative include lookup.
- `BatchProcessFile` opens the destination file only after parsing the source problem succeeds, so a parse failure does not create or truncate the destination. Rust's concrete filesystem method preserves that ordering.
- `BatchProcessFile` contains commented-out Started/Ended status lines but still flushes `stdout` after creating the scanner. Rust now models that standalone flush through the explicit batch global-output writer while leaving the commented status lines dormant unless byte-level runner traces show they matter.
- `BatchProcessVariants` mutates `spec->executable` to the current prover for each variant and restores it after the round. Rust passes the prover through the staged job record; the final runner bridge should choose deliberately between temporary mutation for compatibility and explicit per-job executable routing.
- `BatchProcessVariants` contains a CASC-28/J10 hack that allocates and initializes a fresh `StructFOFSpec` for every concrete problem instead of sharing one initialized spec across the variant round. Rust stages only the orchestration now; real include/problem loading should preserve this no-shared-axioms behavior until variant traces prove it can be simplified.
- `BatchProcessVariants` does not stop the outer variant loop when all abstract problems are solved; later rounds still print "already solved" lines. Rust preserves that loop shape in the staged scheduler, even though a cleaned scheduler would likely break early.
- `BatchProcessVariants` child success is based on the captured SZS result being Theorem or Unsatisfiable, not on the child exit status and not on Satisfiable/CounterSatisfiable statuses. Rust preserves that LTB-specific success set in the generic-process bridge.
- The generic child bridge prints the child controller startup and completion lines before replaying captured child stdout, matching the C parent-side order. A later streaming worker API should preserve that order if byte-level variant traces depend on it.
- `BatchProcessProblem` relies on `EPCtrlSetGetResult(procs, true)` for failed child cleanup and `EPCtrlSetFree(procs, true)` for final cleanup, so temporary-file lifetime is coupled to process-set lifetime. Rust exposes that coupling in `BatchProcCtrlRunnerSet`; a later scheduler API can split process polling from temporary-file ownership after byte-compatible traces exist.
- `batch_create_runner` writes type declarations for the whole signature before the filtered selected problem, not only declarations reachable from selected clauses/formulas. Rust preserves that broad declaration pass for now; a later minimized-problem writer should only be considered after child-runner compatibility tests cover typed LTB problems.
- `BatchProcessProblem` bases its spawn-capacity check on `EPCtrlSetCardinality(procs)`, which reflects failed children deleted by `EPCtrlSetGetResult`. Rust's backend path keeps this as a live `active_count()` query rather than counting historical spawn metadata; later scheduler cleanup can make that distinction explicit in a narrower process-pool abstraction.

Implemented behavior:

- Initial PCL2 `pcl_analysis` support is ported as `src/pcl2/analysis.rs`, including expression/step/protocol proof-distance computation, cached `proof_distance` updates, generation and simplification reference counter updates by C operator class, proof/non-proof reference counter split, example selection by proof-step priority and useless-generation/useless-simplification ratio, and the C loop behavior for zero negative-example budget.
- Initial PCL2 `pcl_lemmas` support is ported as `src/pcl2/lemmas.rs`, including default lemma parameters, deterministic inference weights, active/passive generation and simplification reference counters by C operator class, pure quote references, proof-tree-size caching with lemma-as-zero recursion, lemma-quality computation and comparison, and sequential, recursive, and flat lemma selection with the C sequential limit check shape.
- Initial PCL2 `pcl_propanalysis` support is ported as `src/pcl2/propanalysis.rs`, including max-step selection by standard weight, strict symbol count, literal count, and depth; aggregate FOF/positive/negative/mixed clause counts; literal and symbol-type totals; empty-clause exclusion from global counts; and C-shaped property-summary rendering using the ported clause property-info formatter.
- Initial PCL2 `pcl_proofcheck` support is ported as `src/pcl2/proofcheck.rs`, including check/prover enums, clausal precondition collection, copied parent-clause insertion into generated check problems, negated skolemized target-unit generation, FOF-parent/target warning side-channel rendering through explicit warning-aware APIs, E/TPTP problem rendering and argument-vector invocation, Otter problem rendering and stdin invocation, SPASS/DFG problem rendering and argument-vector invocation, output-level progress/prover-trace/failed-problem rendering through explicit output APIs, assumption and split-step classification, and protocol-level checked/unchecked counting.
- A root Rust package with library and `eprover` binary targets.
- Generic binary-tree traversal support from `clb_avlgeneric`, including left-spine initialization, iterative in-order next-node traversal, empty-root handling, and a function-shaped API matching the generated C traversal pair.
- The `DStr` byte-buffer behavior from `clb_dstrings`, including append, byte-buffer append, integer append, string-array append, last-character deletion, reset, minimize, line reading, and the distinct C growth rules for string and byte appends.
- The `PStack` and `DStack` growth/access patterns from `clb_pstacks` and `clb_dstacks`, including explicit logical capacity doubling, reset without shrinking, top/below-top/element access, swap-remove discard, stack copying/pushing, C-shaped binary search and merge behavior, and integer average/deviation computation.
- Dynamic `PDArray` and `DDArray` storage from `clb_pdarrays` and `clb_ddarrays`, including exponential and fixed-multiple growth, zero/`NULL` initialization, mutating element access that extends arrays like the C macros, delete/store/add/increment helpers, and `DDArraySelectPart` partition selection.
- `PDRangeArr` signed-index dynamic arrays from `clb_pdrangearrays`, including low/limit key tracking, upward and downward expansion, C-compatible offset shifts, pointer-member counting, deletion, copying, and integer increment helpers.
- `FixedDArray` fixed-size integer vector helpers from `clb_fixdarrays`, including initialization, component-wise add/subtract/weighted-add/min/max, copying, and C-shaped debug printing.
- Shared `IntOrP` integer/pointer payload shape from `clb_defines`, represented as a checked Rust enum for containers that mix long integer tags and pointer-like handles.
- `IntMap` multi-representation integer map behavior from `clb_intmap`, including empty/single/array/tree states, density-triggered representation switching, `get_ref` slot creation with null values, assignment and deletion return behavior, entry-count inflation for repeated null array references, inclusive sorted range iteration over non-null values, and debug printing.
- Memory allocation facade behavior from `clb_memory` and `clb_newmem`, including secure allocation/reallocation counters, exact-size and 16-byte aligned free-list policies, newmem chunk population, secure string duplication with trailing NUL bytes, zero-initialized integer arrays, free-list flushing, and debug/free-list stats rendering.
- `MinHeap` binary minimum heap behavior from `clb_min_heap`, including comparator-driven ordering, integer/pointer-shaped add helpers, minimum pop, size/peek queries, update/remove operations, the C helper directions for `decr_key` and `incr_key`, optional index-setter callbacks after swaps/removals, and debug printing.
- Object tree/map behavior from `clb_objtrees` and `clb_objmaps`, including comparison-based object storage, duplicate-preserving object insertions, mutable root-like lookups, object extraction/root extraction, unique merges, map store/get-ref/find/extract semantics, null-value map slots, sorted traversal, and explicit free-with-deleter hooks.
- Partial-ordering helpers from `clb_partial_orderings`, including exact `CompareResult` and `HoOrderKind` discriminants, quasi-order conversion, inverse relation handling, and the C comparison-symbol table.
- Permanent string registry behavior from `clb_permastrings`, including duplicate interning, owned-string store, null-shaped optional lookup, explicit global registry clearing, and returned shared strings that remain valid for Rust holders after the registry is cleared.
- `PList` doubly linked list behavior from `clb_plist`, including explicit anchor cells, insertion after arbitrary list cells, extraction and reinsertion across anchors, deletion, clearing/freeing anchors, freed cell-slot reuse, forward/backward navigation, and mixed integer/pointer payload helpers.
- `PLocalStack` local pointer stack behavior from `clb_plocalstacks`, including caller-managed push capacity, the C `ensure_space` equality-triggered growth rule, old-size-plus-space doubling, term-argument order helpers, and non-raw-pointer tagged-stack slot accounting.
- `PQueue` circular pointer/integer queue behavior from `clb_pqueue`, including head/tail indexing, FIFO extraction, stack-view last extraction, bury-at-front insertion, C-shaped full-ring growth layout, reset without shrinking, absolute tail/index iteration, and mixed integer/pointer helper values.
- Property-bit helpers from `clb_properties`, including set/delete/flip/assign, all-bit and any-bit queries, masked property extraction, and masked equivalence checks.
- Shared simple helpers from `clb_simple_stuff`, including bytewise string distance, weighted-object comparison/sorting, C-shaped JKISS random state and static-state wrapper behavior, bounded indentation strings, prefix tests, null-terminated string-index/cardinality helpers, positive-only GCD, `ProverResult`, `ProblemType`, first-order/higher-order syntax conflict checks, and an executable-run reset helper for isolating C-shaped process-global problem-type state across in-process Rust runs.
- `StrTree` string-keyed map behavior from `clb_stringtrees`, including duplicate-preserving store semantics, lookup, mutable value rewrite, extraction, deletion, and deterministic sorted traversal.
- `FloatTree` floating-point-keyed map behavior from `clb_floattrees`, including duplicate-preserving store semantics, lookup, mutable value rewrite, extraction/deletion, node queries, deterministic sorted traversal for ordered float keys, signed-zero equivalence, infinities, and deterministic NaN bucketing.
- `NumTree` numeric-keyed map behavior from `clb_numtrees`, including duplicate-preserving store semantics, lookup, mutable value rewrite, extraction/deletion, root-like draining, node/max-key queries, debug printing, deterministic sorted traversal, and limited traversal starting at the first key greater than or equal to the limit.
- `NumXTree` numeric-keyed four-slot value map behavior from `clb_numxtrees`, including duplicate-preserving store semantics with defaulted extra slots, full-entry insertion, lookup, mutable value-slot rewrite, extraction/deletion, root-like draining, node/max-key queries, deterministic sorted traversal, and limited traversal starting at the first key greater than or equal to the limit.
- `PTree` pointer/identity-set behavior from `clb_ptrees`, including duplicate-preserving store semantics, lookup, binary lookup alias, extraction/deletion/root-like draining, destructive and non-destructive merge helpers, stack/vector conversion, copying, shared-element and intersection helpers, equivalence/subset checks, in-order visits, and debug printing.
- `QuadTree` four-part pointer/integer-keyed map behavior from `clb_quadtrees`, including pointer-then-integer pair comparison, duplicate-preserving insertion, mutable root-like lookup, extraction/deletion, deterministic sorted traversal, and mixed integer/pointer payload helpers.
- Registered persistent-memory helpers from `clb_regmem`, including process-global registration, allocation/reallocation/freeing, cleanup of all registered areas, `RegMemProvide` doubling behavior, and zero-filled newly provided tails.
- `SysDate` logical-time helpers from `clb_sysdate`, including creation/invalid sentinels, ordering/equality/maximum macros, increment mutation order, overflow reporting instead of undefined signed overflow, and C-shaped unsigned-long printing.
- Verbose-level helpers from `clb_verbose`, including the global `Verbose` default, nonzero level-1 gate, `>= 2` and `>= 10` gates, closure helpers for macro-like conditional execution, and `VERBOUT`/`VERBOUTARG` formatting with explicit flushes.
- Compact clause-position helpers from `ccl_clausecpos`, including weighted term-position packing, weighted clause-position packing over literal standard weights and right-side offsets, unpacking into full `ClausePos` cursors, compact-position subterm lookup, first/next literal compact-position iteration, and relative literal-position splitting.
- Initial new-evaluation helpers from `ccl_neweval`, including exact priority constants, process-global evaluation counter allocation, C-shaped simple evaluation cells with float heuristic storage and per-position tree-link slots, evaluation-list priority mutation, print/comment string formatting, C comparison/greater-than tie-break behavior including NaN handling through the `CMP` macro shape, and handle-backed splay-tree insertion, lookup, extraction, deletion, smallest lookup, in-order traversal, and debug printing.
- Clause-local and plain-set helpers from `ccl_clausefunc`, including index-based literal removal with cached count/weight updates, literal sign flipping with cached polarity counts, duplicate and trivially false literal cleanup with `DCNormalize` derivation metadata, plain clause-set cleanup with cached literal recounting, plain clause-set canonization and structural-weight sorting, AC-resolved negative literal cleanup, non-orientable unit simplify-reflect tests, naked Boolean-variable elimination through temporary bindings, C-shaped `DCNormalize` metadata, and true-literal tautology creation, term-formula clause collection/CNF set insertion helpers, higher-order flex-clause resolution with `DCFlexResolve`, compact-parent `ClauseIsOrphaned` and `ClauseSetDeleteOrphans` predicate behavior, injectivity-definition recognition/replacement with inverse typed-Skolem generation and duplicate-definition renaming checks, beta-normal represented choice-axiom recognition with proof-state `choice_opcodes` storage, and canonical clause comparison by structural weight.
- Clause source-info metadata from `ccl_clauseinfo`, including optional copied name/source fields, empty `-1` line/column allocation, TSTP/PCL source rendering with unknown and line/column fallbacks, null-info no-op rendering, and C-shaped generated identifier namespace/counter extraction.
- PCL/TSTP proof-documentation step helpers from `ccl_inferencedoc`, including the `PCLTypeStr` mappings for conjecture/question/negated-conjecture, the empty default string for plain/axiom and other roles, C-shaped compact/non-compact `pcl_print_start` prefixes, `pcl_print_end` comment/watchlist suffix handling, `tstp_print_end` comment/watchlist suffix handling for initial clause documentation, formula-side `pcl_formula_print_start` plus `pcl_formula_print_end`/`tstp_formula_print_end` comment terminators, reusable clause inference-expression rendering for binary/unary clause inferences, AC-resolution axiom lists, rewrite chains, and clause definition-application chains, reusable formula inference-expression rendering for introduction, parent-based formula transformations, and nested definition application, exact `OutputFormatType` discriminants, a `DocClauseCreation`-style session helper for initial, paramodulation, simultaneous-paramodulation, equality-resolution, equality-factoring, factoring, and split creation documentation in PCL/TSTP with optional TSTP term type suffixes, C's unsupported-format fallback, and PCL initial-clause stdout marker interleaving metadata, a `DocClauseModification`-style session helper for simplify-reflect, contextual simplify-reflect, AC-resolution, condensation, minimization, answer-literal evaluation, and destructive equality-resolution documentation, pre-rendered formula-view `DocFormulaCreation`/`DocFormulaModification`-style session helpers for represented formula proof-documentation branches until full `WFormula` ownership is ported, and represented `DocClauseFromForm`, `DocClauseQuote`, `DocClauseRewrite`, `DocClauseEqUnfold`, `DocIntroSplitDef`, `DocIntroSplitDefRest`, `DocClauseApplyDefs`, and `DocFormulaIntroDefs` session helpers.
- Clause-position cursors from `ccl_clausepos`, including semi-initialized allocation, clause-backed and standalone literal positions, designated/other side lookup, subterm lookup through `TermPos`, C-shaped clause-position printing, positive/maximal literal cursor search, maximal-side traversal with unoriented right-side visits, leftmost-innermost maximal-subterm traversal, and rewrite-chain sequence extraction over term rewrite links.
- Clause-position trees from `ccl_clausepos_tree`, including clause-to-compact-position association cells, stable clause-identifier comparison for cloned Rust clause snapshots, duplicate-collapsing position insertion, single-position deletion with empty-cell removal, whole-clause deletion, sorted compact-position storage, compact debug rendering of stored clause identifiers and positions, and default LOP plus explicit LOP/TPTP/TSTP clause-position debug rendering.
- Initial plain clause-set owner helpers from `ccl_clausesets`, including C-shaped creation-date initialization, append-order insertion, first-entry extraction, set-to-set movement, cached member/literal accounting, identifier storage, identifier lookup/extraction/deletion, pointer-identity `ClauseSetFind`, clause-owned evaluation-index roots with `ClauseSetFindBest`/best extraction and conjecture-order scanning, and untyped-set checks.
- Contextual simplify-reflect helpers from `ccl_context_sr`, including C stack-order literal processing, sign-flip before subsumption-order sorting, plain clause-set subsumer lookup, SOS inheritance, `CPInitial`/`CPLimitedRW` clearing, flipped-literal removal with cached-weight updates, `DCContextSR` derivation entries, restoration when no subsumer exists, and `ClauseSetFindContextSRClauses` duplicate-push behavior when multiple flipped literals make the query subsume the same target clause.
- Mixed unit simplification helpers from `ccl_unit_simplify`, including exact `UnitSimplifyType` discriminants and name translation, top-level signed/unsigned unit lookup, full single-difference descent that switches to positive-unit-only subterm lookup after top lookup fails, plain-set `ClauseSimplifyWithUnitSet` behavior for opposite-signed unit cuts, same-signed unit subsumption returning false, `CPLimitedRW`-only clearing on cuts, cached-weight decrement through literal removal, and the C boolean-as-property `CPIsSOS` side effect on subsuming units.
- Weighted axiom sorting helpers from `ccl_axiomsorter`, including exact `AxiomType` discriminants, clause axiom allocation, formula-f-code placeholder allocation until formula payloads are integrated with axiom sorting, relevance evaluation by average nonzero nonspecial symbol relevance with old-weight preservation when no symbol contributes, and weight/type/pointer-identity comparison.
- Initial clause/formula property helpers from `ccl_clauses`, including exact `FormulaProperties` bit values, property set/delete/give/query/any-set operations, TPTP type masking and setting, C's conjecture-role predicate that includes `question`, TPTP type combination precedence, CSSCPA source bit encoding, and clause-type identifier mapping including higher-order `definition` lambda-definition marking.
- Core clause-cell helpers from `ccl_clauses`, including clause allocation with positive literals partitioned before negative literals, cached positive/negative literal counts, property/type/source metadata helpers, nullable evaluation-cell storage with C-copy paths dropping evaluations, literal-count and classification predicates, semantic-false/semantic-empty/triviality checks, maximal positive equation lookup, AC-redundancy checks, equational/pure-equational/ground/untyped predicates, range/anti-range/strong-range restriction checks, equation-definition detection, canonical literal sorting, subsumption-order sorting/checking wrappers, structural and technical comparison helpers, OCB-backed literal orientation/maximality wrappers, conditional and unconditional maximal-term marking through literal orientation/maximality flags, OCB-backed multiset greater/equal rejection with C literal-flag side effects, metadata-preserving copy/flat-copy/optimized/disjoint-copy/skolemized-copy paths, variable normalization through a fresh variable bank, standard/orientation/function/nonlinear/symbol-type clause weight wrappers, term-property forwarding, substitution normalization, depth and symbol/variable/f-code/function-occurrence/subterm/ground-term collection wrappers, literal predicate queries, simple-term-backed `ClauseStartsMaybe`, `ClauseParse`, and `ClausePCLParse` control-flow branches for LOP/TPTP/TSTP/PCL input with C-shaped token source/line/column capture into parsed clause info, first-order clause list/axiom/rule/goal/query/LOP/TPTP/PCL/debug rendering, TSTP-core string rendering, the empty/first-order-untyped `ClauseTSTPPrint` wrapper branch over bank-explicit equation printers, and typed first-order plus represented higher-order non-empty `ClauseTSTPPrint` formula rendering through direct universal closure over the clause literals.
- Condensation helpers from `ccl_condensation`, including process-wide attempt/success counters, single-step condensation through one-way literal unification, substitution-backed copy-except candidate construction, duplicate/resolved literal cleanup, subsumption-order preparation, original-clause literal replacement only when the candidate subsumes the source clause, repeated condensation until a fixed point, and `DCCondense` derivation metadata.

- Clause-function helpers from `ccl_clausefunc`, including default LOP and explicit LOP/TPTP/TSTP `PStackClausePrint` stack-order rendering with optional per-clause suffix text and set-loop newlines.

- The temporary THF bridge also accepts applied function equality such as `f @ a = b` and `b = f @ a`, plus conjunction/disjunction over applied predicates such as `p @ a & q @ a`, by parsing application before the existing equality/connective lowering.
- Beta-reducible THF lambda applications such as `(^[X: person]: p @ X) @ a` now normalize through `NamedToDB`/beta conversion before reaching the temporary clause bridge, so they can participate in supported first-order-shaped THF proof search. Lambda-bearing or DB-indexed terms that remain after normalization still stop at the temporary bridge boundary with the explicit full higher-order formula-pipeline diagnostic.
- Typed quantified THF bodies can now use simple predicate application, for example `![X: person]: p @ X` and existential conjectures such as `?[X: person]: p @ X`, through the same temporary clause bridge; full higher-order quantified formula ownership is still deferred.
- Unary negation in the temporary THF bridge now consumes a following simple application before building the negation, so `~ p @ a` is treated like `~(p @ a)` for the supported first-order-shaped application fragment.
- Simple non-Boolean THF application arguments can now themselves be applications, so fragments such as `p @ (f @ a)` parse and lower through the temporary clause bridge.
- The same THF bridge preserves left-associative application when a bare argument has arrow type or when a bare Boolean argument is itself an application, so `appfun @ f @ a` is parsed as `(appfun @ f) @ a` and `r @ p @ a` is rejected instead of being treated as `r @ (p @ a)`; the parenthesized form remains supported.
- Parenthesized THF logical operator heads keep their declared arrow type through application parsing, so forms such as `(&) @ (p @ a) @ (q @ a)` parse as conjunctions instead of failing after the zero-arity logical head is manufactured; parenthesized Boolean operands now stop at the matching `)` so the caller's next `@` remains part of the outer application chain.
- THF application formulas now reuse the term-bank TSTP formula precedence layer for mixed fragments such as `p @ a & q @ a => r @ a`, parsing conjunction before disjunction and allowing one following non-associative connective before the temporary CNF bridge lowers the result.
- The temporary FOF/TFF/TCF and old `input_formula(...)` bridge also accepts C-supported formula-level `=` and `!=` when a compound formula operand leaves those tokens for the formula parser, lowering them through the existing equivalence and XOR CNF paths while ordinary term equality remains handled by the equation parser.
- The temporary FOF/TFF/TCF bridge now accepts top-level Boolean `$ite(...)` and `$let(...)` formula atoms through the same term-bank path C uses before predicate-literal encoding, including as unparenthesized existential bodies, formula-level equality/disequality can treat those FOOL atoms as equivalence/XOR operands instead of prematurely consuming them as term equalities, non-Boolean `$ite`/`$let` primary terms followed by `=` or `!=` remain ordinary typed equality/disequality literals instead of being rejected by the formula-primary branch, semantic clause lowering expands direct formula-position Boolean `$ite(C,T,F)` predicate literals plus the first direct-literal term-position `$ite` occurrence, rewrites direct complex-Boolean `$eq`/`$neq` formula terms to equivalence/XOR or the C `$true` shortcut before Boolean-term conversion only inside the `--fool-unroll` phase, and when `--fool-unroll` is enabled it expands the first direct-literal nontrivial Boolean formula subterm in literal arguments into C's guarded implication shapes; direct `$let` occurrences through supported quantifier/connective scopes are lifted into fresh `epred`/`esk` definitions before CNF, and conjecture lowering now negates the rewritten body while lowering generated definitions positively so they are not distributed into the negated conjecture formula or stamped with the input wrapper's role/source metadata, syntax-only `--print-formulas` follows the configured `--fool-unroll` value for this bridge split, `--app-encode` preserves the original term-encoded `$ite`/`$let`, and full formula ownership is still required before this is treated as complete FOOL support.
- The temporary bridge's direct Boolean-subterm split and direct complex-Boolean `$eq`/`$neq` replacement are gated by `--fool-unroll`, while direct `$ite` and `$let` lifting preserve encoded Boolean equalities when unrolling is disabled. `$let` internals remain a separate let-lifting concern rather than ordinary FOOL subterms, matching the current staged pass boundary.
- Supported THF/FOOL `$let` formulas with parameterized definitions now treat definition-head variables as let-local formals during the bridge's TSTP free-variable rejection and Skolem dependency scans, so direct applications such as `$let(f: s > $o, f(X) := p @ X, f @ a)` lower through the current CNF bridge in both axiom and conjecture roles instead of being rejected as globally free.
