Known gaps:

- `pcl_analysis` reports dangling proof-distance references as syntax diagnostics rather than reproducing C's null dereference, preserves silent ignores for dangling direct references during generation/simplification counter updates, uses deterministic PCL-id ordering for equal example-selection scores instead of qsort's unspecified tie order, and keeps f32-shaped example scoring plus the C `neg_examples == 0` no-selection loop behavior.
- `pcl_lemmas` uses deterministic zero defaults for C's uninitialized inference-weight slots, preserves the executable formula that ignores `proof_tree_w`/`proof_dag_w`, preserves the sequential selector's post-mark `res > max_number` limit check, and reports missing proof-size parents as syntax diagnostics while keeping missing reference-counter parents non-fatal.
- `pcl_propanalysis` keeps C's zero-denominator average formulas and split between empty-clause exclusion from aggregate counts and inclusion in max-step scans, but avoids C's invalid union dereferences for empty, FOF-only, or shell-containing protocols.
- `pcl_proofcheck` currently ports generated check-problem construction, E/Otter/SPASS problem rendering and process invocation, FOF-parent/target warning side-channel rendering, output-level progress/prover-trace/failed-problem rendering, and check bookkeeping. C's shell-string `popen` execution, fixed-line output scanning, clausal-only handling of FOF parents, legacy Otter/SPASS truth-literal hacks, and unchecked-as-assumed split-step reporting are documented for later cleanup.
- The prover core, full parser/formula printing, clausification, saturation loop, integrated indexing, ordering, full heuristic control, proof output, resource limits, and most CLI option effects are not implemented yet.
- Scanner support includes both automatic `include_key` stream splicing and explicit `ScannerParseInclude`-style include parsing, but higher-level formula parsers still need to dispatch included scanners.
- `InitIO` stores the program name for later diagnostics, but Rust diagnostic rendering still mostly passes program names explicitly until the executable-level fatal-error path is completed.
- `IntMap` preserves the C representation-state decisions but uses safe Rust-owned storage; the C implementation's hidden `PDRangeArr` growth during some read/delete paths and inflated null-slot entry counts should be evaluated later as compatibility risks versus cleanup opportunities.
- `Memory`/`NewMem` expose safe byte-buffer allocation and accounting rather than raw `malloc` pointers; the freelist and chunk policies are modeled for sizing/reuse behavior, but allocator-address identity and true uninitialized memory are intentionally not exposed.
- `PLocalTaggedStack` models the portable non-`TAGGED_POINTERS` two-slot representation; raw low-bit pointer tagging is intentionally not used in safe Rust and should be reconsidered only if profiling proves the extra slot accounting matters.
- `PQueue` preserves the observable circular-buffer behavior, but the C implementation exposes `PQueueGrow` even though its copy logic is only valid after a store/bury creates a full ring; the Rust port keeps growth internal until a real direct caller appears.
- `CompareResult::NotGreaterEqual` and `CompareResult::NotLessEqual` are cache-only C enum values with no entries in `POCompareSymbol`; Rust returns `None` for those symbols instead of allowing out-of-bounds array access.
- `StrTree` currently uses Rust `BTreeMap` to preserve sorted traversal and lookup semantics; the C implementation's splay-tree locality optimization should be revisited if profiling shows this index on hot paths.
- `FloatTree` currently uses Rust `BTreeMap` with an internal total-order float key; exact C splay-root locality and C's accidental behavior for NaN keys are not modeled beyond deterministic handling.
- `NumTree` currently uses Rust `BTreeMap` for the same reason; exact splay-root locality is not modeled beyond tracking a recent root-like key for extraction/draining.
- `NumXTree` currently uses Rust `BTreeMap` for the same reason; exact splay-root locality is not modeled beyond tracking a recent root-like key for extraction/draining.
- `ObjTree` and `ObjMap` model comparison-function behavior with `Ord` keys and safe owned values; exact C splay-root locality, raw pointer ownership transfer, and allocator reuse are not modeled.
- OS wrapper helpers from `clb_os_wrapper`, including resource-limit result discriminants, Linux `getrlimit`/`setrlimit` soft/hard-limit wrappers for the current resource-limit surface, Windows Job Object process-memory limits behind safe wrappers, cooperative Windows CPU-deadline support through `Kernel32` process-time queries instead of native process-time quota termination, wall-clock microsecond/millisecond/second helpers, Linux `clock()` and Windows `Kernel32` process CPU-time queries behind narrow unsafe external-library boundaries, Windows page-size/physical-memory/peak-working-set queries, Linux `sysconf` core-count/page-size/physical-memory queries with existing fallbacks, Linux `getrusage` resource-footer counters with `/proc` fallback, monotonic process-relative fallback clock for unsupported targets, memory striding, C `fopen` mode mapping, and secure open/close diagnostics.
- `PTree` currently uses Rust `BTreeSet` for deterministic ordered set semantics; exact C splay-root locality and pointer-address ordering should be revisited once stable arena/handle identity is wired into terms.
- `QuadTree` currently uses Rust `BTreeMap` and caller-provided ordered pointer identities; exact C splay-root locality and raw pointer-address ordering should be revisited when comparison caches are integrated with term storage.
- `RegMem` uses explicit opaque handles and initialized byte buffers instead of raw registered pointers and uninitialized `SecureMalloc` memory; call sites that need typed static scratch space should wrap those handles at the ownership boundary.
- `SysDate` uses an LP64-shaped `i64` raw value to match the WSL/Linux C reference build; if a native Windows C reference becomes relevant, C `long` width and `SysDatePrint` output need a target-specific audit.
- Verbose output helpers are wired into the executable's parsed `--verbose` option, but future progress-reporting call sites still need to use them as the parser/prover pipeline is ported.
- The executable run path applies output-level state, redirects initial print-info output, parses supported clause-list inputs into `ProofState`, loads configured watchlist file/inline state for that clause-list path, initializes proof control, emits C-style initial AC scan/status comments during proof-state initialization when AC handling is enabled, runs the staged first-order saturation loop, and maps proof/saturation/limit outcomes to C-style exit statuses. Full `GlobalOut`-style routing through parsing, preprocessing, proof search, and proof/statistics printing still awaits the complete proof pipeline.
- `--cpu-limit`, `--soft-cpu-limit`, and `--memory-limit` update the C-shaped hard, soft, schedule, memory, and derived heuristic-limit state. Supported proof-search runs now arm a cooperative CPU-clock deadline from the soft limit when present, otherwise the hard limit; soft deadline expiry returns the C-shaped user-resource outcome and `RESOURCE_OUT` exit status, while hard deadline expiry now returns through the hard CPU-limit finalizer with C's doubled-`COMCHAR` `ResourceOut` banner, stderr diagnostic, no-output-file direct-banner-before-buffered-stdout ordering, and `CPU_LIMIT_ERROR` exit status. Linux builds now expose C-shaped `getrlimit`/`setrlimit` soft/hard/full-limit wrappers, install the `SIGXCPU` handler in normal builds, apply C's initial `RLIMIT_CPU` choice (soft limit when present, otherwise hard limit), warn on reduced/failed CPU-limit setup with the C `SetSoftRlimitErr` message shapes including `strerror(errno)` text for unmasked failures, print C's separate `perror("eprover")` line before warning when core-dump suppression cannot be applied, capture the system hard CPU limit during signal setup, reset `RLIMIT_CPU` to that system limit on a soft timeout signal before rearming the hard limit, expose C's scheduler SIGTERM count-plus-default-reset handler shape, and apply the C `RLIMIT_DATA` memory behavior including the duplicated `RLIMIT_AS` branch bug that still passes `RLIMIT_DATA` and the failed-`RLIMIT_DATA` warning mask; native Windows keeps finite `--cpu-limit` on the cooperative process-CPU deadline because `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_PROCESS_TIME` terminates with `STATUS_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` before C-shaped timeout output can be emitted, while nonzero `--memory-limit` still uses a retained Job Object process-memory limit when the current process can be assigned to one, warning on setup failures. Hard-limit signal termination after soft-limit expiry, real process exit from the hard signal path, and signal-time descriptor writes remain pending with the platform shutdown abstraction. `--resources-info` writes the C preprocessing-time and footer shapes using `os_wrapper`; native Windows reports current-process user/system CPU time and peak working-set pages via `Kernel32`, and Linux now uses `getrusage(RUSAGE_SELF/RUSAGE_CHILDREN)` to sum user/system time while preserving C's parent-only raw `ru_maxrss` field under the historic "pages" label, with `/proc` fallback if the POSIX call fails. Exact maximum-resident-set unit matching remains target-dependent.
- Supported executable comment output now uses the reference WSL/Linux C build's `COMCHAR` raw prefix `%` for preprocessing diagnostics, result banners, SZS statuses, proof-object framing, answer tuples, proof-state section headings, statistics, and resource footer lines. C can be compiled with `UNIX_COMMENTS` to switch `COMCHAR` to `#`; Rust should make that an explicit compatibility mode only if such a build becomes a supported target.
- Proof-output options such as `--proof-object`, `--proof-graph`, `--proof-statistics`, `--full-deriv`, `--force-deriv`, `--record-gcs`, and `--training-examples` are parsed and recorded. For supported proof-found clause-list runs, `--proof-object=1` now emits the C `SZS output start/end CNFRefutation` framing with represented clause ancestors before the proof root, represented processed/unprocessed `--full-deriv` roots when requested, C-shaped PCL/TSTP derivation or source-info payloads, final/proof root markers only on roots, and display-only sequential ids with represented clause-parent and rewrite-demodulator refs remapped for the printed list; for complete no-proof clause-list runs, it emits the C `Saturation` proof-object framing with represented processed roots plus reachable clause ancestors, including clause-side PCL/TSTP derivation or source-info payloads, final/proof root markers only on roots, and display-only sequential ids/remapped parent refs. `--force-deriv=1/2` now enables represented stopped-outcome proof objects for supported incomplete/resource clause-list runs when a proof-output format is selected, uses C's `Derivation` status, expands represented list ancestors, adds unprocessed roots at level 2, supports `--proof-statistics` without list/graph output, and suppresses later saturated-state printing at level 2 like C. `--proof-graph` now emits represented clause-side GraphViz DOT graphs for supported proof-success and complete no-proof clause-list runs, using C-like sequential node ids to avoid archive-copy identifier collisions; graph modes above level 1 render C-shaped TSTP clause labels plus represented clause derivations or source-info labels, proof-success DOT uses the same represented `--full-deriv` roots as list/statistics mode, and graph edges inherit the child node colour like C. `--proof-object=0` preserves C's accepted no-output surface when no earlier proof-output option enabled proof objects. Supported proof-success runs now mark represented clause ancestors before C-style GC analysis, so `--print-statistics --record-gcs` reports represented proof-search given-clause usage, and `--training-examples` prints the represented count/section/list output with C's positive/negative selector bits and selected LOP/TPTP/TSTP clause rendering. `--proof-statistics` now prints the C proof-object counter block for represented proof-success, complete saturation, and forced stopped roots whenever a proof object is enabled, including the no-list `--record-gcs`/`--force-deriv` cases and represented proof-success `--full-deriv` roots. The marker, statistics traversal, graph traversal, and proof-object list traversal prefer selected archive copies for proof-step parents and original quote sources for `DCCnfQuote` parents when compact Rust references collide with original axioms, collapse only literal-identical dummy quote copies, and keep mutated quoted clauses as derived proof-object nodes until C-like parent identity is represented. Formula-aware derivation extraction, extraction-root selection beyond represented returned/full-derivation/complete-saturation/forced stopped clauses, exact C topological sorting, exact C renumbering, full ordered PCL/TSTP derived-step expansion beyond represented clause roots, formula-aware DOT labels, formula-archive traversal in proof objects, AC auxiliary proof-stat/graph roots and DOT label parents, and full pointer-stable proof-object marking still await the full proof pipeline.

Exact pointer-like parent identity still awaits stable proof-state clause handles instead of compact `ident/source` references.
- `che_clausefeatures` full call-site integration remains pending until every executable path that printed through C globals passes an explicit output format. Timeout clausification/classification remains pending. The reusable non-printing clause-set counters, arity summaries, caller-rendered/default/format-aware selective line wrappers, proof-state selective printing over represented clause/type/equality sections, C-shaped limits/classes, cheap threshold classification, feature-vector string formatting/parsing, type-string encoding, limit printing, callback-backed higher-order feature aggregate, and proof-state choice-axiom recognition hook are ported first.
- `che_rawspecfeatures` `RawSpecFeaturesCompute` now covers represented `ProofState` clause and formula owner fields: clause/formula axiom cardinality, clause/formula standard weight with C's lambda-definition exclusion, clause/formula conjecture and hypothesis counts, signature predicate/function arity counts, choice-symbol scan, formula-only order and conjecture-order scans, formula definition/lambda/applied-variable statistics over active and archived formula axioms, the executable raw-formula bridge adjustments, and class reset. Full parser/preprocessing replacement of the executable raw-formula bridge with exact formula-owner input remains pending.
- `che_specsigfeatures` term, clause, plain clause-set, and print-vector helpers are ported. The upstream file comments mention "later" formula sets but export only term/clause/clause-set collectors in this checkout, so Rust should not invent formula-set collection without a C reference or a cleaned-design decision.
- `che_termweights` related-term-set consumers beyond relative term, prefix, TF-IDF, and conjecture Lev/Struc/Tree distance weights remain pending until relevance filtering and the heuristic-control callers are wired. The tuple helpers, generalization builders, term-bank frequency counters, and ordered negated-conjecture collector are ported first.
- `che_axiomscan` sets associative/commutative signature properties and records newly detected AC clauses in a compact `sig->ac_axioms`-style signature list. Its C-shaped return value reports commutativity detection as AC activation while associativity-only scans still mutate the signature and return false.
- Initial `ccl_bce` blocked-clause elimination helper, including C-shaped predicate occurrence tracking with max-occurrence cutoffs that disable both polarities, per-literal task creation over disjoint parent copies, minimum-remaining-candidate scheduling, blocker resumption when offending clauses move to the archive, non-equational L-resolvent tautology checks through first-order MGU and complementary-literal closure, equational same-head L-resolvent construction through generated argument disequalities, and C-shaped `% BCE start` / `% BCE eliminated` output. - Initial `ccl_pred_elim` singular predicate-elimination helper with gate recognition disabled, including C-shaped predicate task collection, permanent max-occurrence and conjecture-symbol blocking, offending-clause scheduling exclusion, first-order non-equational resolvents over disjoint positive-parent copies, C's global equality-resolver switch when any equality literal is present, equality-aware argument-disequality resolvents, distinct-variable pivot instantiation, tautology filtering, `DCPEResolve` derivation metadata, C's literal/clause/mu decrease test, archive movement of eliminated clauses, generated-clause variable normalization, and C-shaped `% PE start` / `% PE eliminated` output. Supported first-order prune/proof-search `--pred-elim` preprocessing is wired after BCE and before goal-definition transformation when gate recognition is disabled, including supported first-order-shaped THF formula fragments after clause lowering. Gate/PicoSAT recognition, offending-clause gate elimination, and stable pointer-like proof-state handles remain pending.
- Initial `ccl_gd_transformation` goal-definition helper, including conjecture-clause ground-term collection by selected literal sign, non-constant filtering, recursive subterm definition mode, definition-normal-form rewriting through already introduced definitions, fresh typed `edef` constant allocation, positive unit equality insertion into the same clause set, and `DCIntroDef` derivation metadata. Supported prune/proof-search `--goal-defs` and `--goal-subterm-defs` preprocessing is wired after BCE and before initial docs/watchlist/proof-control initialization; full formula-owner preprocessing, exact C pointer-order term traversal, and stable proof-state handles remain pending.
- Initial `ccl_unfold_defs` coverage includes the `ClauseSetPreprocess` subset with superfluous literal removal, ground-completion tautology filtering through the temporary term bank, optional represented injectivity-definition replacement, and clause-set canonization. It also includes first-order and represented higher-order `ClauseUnfoldEqDef`, `ClauseSetUnfoldEqDef`, `ClauseSetUnfoldAllEqDefs`, and `ClauseSetUnfoldEqDefNormalize`, with definition-archive movement, `DCUnfold` derivation metadata, `ClauseExtractHODefinition`-style symbol/lambda extraction, named-lambda-to-DB conversion, tautology refiltering, canonization after successful unfolding, and the `eqdef_incrlimit`/`eqdef_maxclauses` gates. Supported prune/proof-search runs copy active clause axioms into `ax_archive`, then apply the preprocessing subset before BCE/GD unless `--no-preprocessing` is set, then apply equality-definition normalization regardless of `--no-preprocessing` unless `--no-eq-unfolding` or the size gate disables it, and proof statistics report the combined removed clause count. Inline watchlist clauses that were parsed with the input and configured file-watchlist clauses are passed as the passive set for this normalization before watchlist initial documentation. Live `DocClauseEqUnfold` stdout emission and formula-backed equality-definition call-site coverage remain pending.
- `ccl_context_sr` now has an opt-in documenting helper for `DocClauseModification(inf_context_simplify_reflect, subsumer)`, and proof-control forward-contraction wrappers can thread it through contextual simplify-reflect. Other executable call sites still need proof-session ownership. The plain Rust helpers preserve the clause mutation, sorting, subsumption, deletion, duplicate-discovery behavior, and `DCContextSR` derivation entry.
- `ccl_unit_simplify` still uses a plain clause-set scan rather than `unit_set->demod_index`/`PDTreeFindNextDemodulator`; replace the linear lookup with the indexed demodulator path once clause-set demodulator-index ownership and live `ClausePos` handles are available. Proof-documentation output from unit cuts/subsumption also remains deferred at call sites beyond the ported simplify-reflect modification renderer.
- `che_to_params` warning emission for missing ordered config fields is represented as a parse report instead of directly printing through C's global `Warning()` sink; wire this into the eventual Rust warning/output layer when heuristic configuration files are parsed by the executable.
- `che_to_autoselect` `OrderEvaluate` is available over an explicit mutable axiom clause set, and `TOCreateOrdering` is available as a Rust `to_create_ordering` bridge over the current signature/axiom inputs, including LPO/KBO allocation, generated or predefined precedence installation, generated KBO weights, predefined-only matrix-backed precedence-dependent KBO weights, user weight overrides, and typed literal-comparison validation. `OrderFindOptimal` and the optimizing branch of `TOSelectOrdering` are ported over an explicit mutable term bank and axiom set, including C-shaped candidate seeding, `OrderNextOrdering` iteration, lower-score replacement, maximal-term scoring side effects, and `rewrite_strong_rhs_inst` propagation. The non-optimizing `TOSelectOrdering` path is also available as `to_select_ordering`, including C-shaped `NoOrdering`/constant-weight normalization. The initialized `generate_auto_ordering`, `generate_autocasc_ordering`, `generate_autodev_ordering`, and `generate_autosched0_ordering` through `generate_autosched9_ordering` paths are represented by `generate_auto_ordering` over `AutoOrderingMode`; Rust initializes the full parameter cell for CASC/Dev before applying their visible C field assignments.
- `che_to_precgen` can generate sorted precedence arrays, low-to-high symbol orders, and install generated/predefined precedence into an OCB. `POrientAxioms` remains the C-shaped "Not yet implemented" diagnostic, and proof-state auto-ordering still needs the outer `TOGeneratePrecedence` caller integration.
- `che_to_weightgen` can generate pure weight arrays and ordering weight metadata, apply late `TOWeightsParse`-style user overrides to pure generated arrays, parse predefined KBO weight strings into an OCB, install generated weights plus late user overrides into OCBs, and compute precedence-dependent KBO weights from either generated total precedence ranks or the parsed partial OCB matrix. Driving the C global `problemType` directly remains pending until full ordering-control integration. The helper preserves C quirks such as raw constant overrides, possible zero weights from rank sentinels, partial-precedence incomparability during direct/inverse precedence counts, alpha-rank fallback for pre-rank sorting, and LFHO combined-frequency source inconsistencies; revisit those only behind reference strategy tests.
- `cto_kbo` covers the legacy first-order KBO path, `cto_kbolin` covers the first-order linear `KBO6` path plus the LFHO KBO6 branch for higher-order problems, including first-order-shaped terms, visible DB/lambda/phony surfaces, ordinary deref propagation, no-cache bound applied-variable expansion, and comparison-local weak-head beta dereferencing for `DEREF_ALWAYS`, and the no-bank KBO6 `LAMBDA_ORDER` subset for first-order-shaped exposed terms. `cto_lpo` covers standard first-order LPO plus first-order LPO4/copy wrappers, and `cto_lpo_debug` covers the separate first-order debug LPO path. `cto_orderings` dispatches the ported concrete production variants. The full `KBO6` Lambda-order owner-bank beta/eta path, cache-backed/owner-bank LFHO WHNF dereferencing, higher-order LPO4 normalization branches, RPO implementation, and dispatcher support for RPO remain pending before ordering control can drive clause orientation and maximality end to end.
- `cto_ocb` now has the reusable allocation, weight lookup, precedence comparison, tuple closure/backtracking, min-constant/min-term helpers, `OCBTermMaxFunCode` traversal, and OCB debug printing. Full higher-order variable-map behavior and direct proof-state-owned ordering-generation callers beyond the current `ProofControlInit`/`TOSelectOrdering` bridge remain pending. The Rust implementation initializes precedence-weight vectors deterministically and guards distinct-property checks for symbols beyond the live signature while preserving the C observable ordering rules.
- `che_dagweight` and `che_refinedweight` direct `ProofState`/`OCB` ownership remains pending for their refined C compute paths; explicit OCB-backed compute helpers preserve C's conditional maximal-term marking when callers already own a mutable clause, while immutable WFCB callbacks continue to score already-stored flags.
- `che_diversityweight` and `che_orientweight` direct `ProofState`/`OCB` ownership remains pending; their explicit OCB-backed compute helpers preserve C's conditional maximal-term marking when callers already own a mutable clause, while immutable WFCB callbacks continue to score already-stored flags.
- `che_termweight`, `che_prefixweight`, `che_tfidfweight`, `che_levweight`, `che_strucweight`, and `che_treeweight` direct `ProofState` ownership remains pending; the Rust WFCB/parsers use the current axiom-backed parse context and explicit `&TermBank`, with lazy related-conjecture initialization. Their explicit OCB-backed compute helpers preserve C's conditional maximal-term marking when callers already own a mutable clause; the immutable WFCB callback shape should be revisited once HCB/proof-state clause ownership can thread mutable clauses plus OCBs directly.
- `che_funweights` direct `ProofState`/`OCB` ownership remains pending for the generic funweight and symbol-offset compute paths; explicit OCB-backed helpers preserve C's lazy initialization, conditional maximal-term marking, and scoring order when callers already own a mutable clause, while immutable WFCB callbacks continue to score already-stored flags.
- `che_varweights` direct `ProofState`/`OCB` ownership remains pending for its C compute paths that orient clauses while scoring; explicit OCB-backed helpers preserve the C conditional maximal-term marking before type/signature/proof/depth/non-linear/PN-refined/symbol-type scoring when callers already own a mutable clause, while immutable WFCB callbacks continue to score already-stored flags.
- `che_hcb` low-level selection helpers keep an injected orphan predicate, and proof-control now supplies a source-aware compact-parent live/dead snapshot for default selected-clause extraction and cleanup. Stable clause handles still need to replace compact `ident/source` refs before proof-object traversal and archived/requeued duplicate identifiers are fully disambiguated.
- `che_hcbadmin` parsing can build HCB schedules over the existing Rust WFCB-admin handles, but full executable/proof-control installation and the C `OCB`/`ProofState` parser context remain pending; Rust currently threads only the available WFCB `WeightParseContext`.
- `che_heuristics` now has the standalone `GetHeuristic` lookup/inline-definition path and a pure `finalize_auto_parms` helper that copies heuristic parameters, derives `delete_bad_limit` from `mem_limit` with the C single-precision cast shape, and disables AC handling when `SpecNoEq` holds. `ProofControl` can now hold the converted heuristic parameter cell, executable `--select-strategy` applies predefined `schedule.vars` strategies before proof-control initialization, `--print-strategy` prints the current, named, all-name, and all-strategy surfaces without entering proof search, and supported first-order `--auto` runs select the generated preprocessing and search strategies with C-shaped class/configuration comments, the C no-filter Auto SInE report, and C's hyphenated `DEFAULT_MASK` search class while replaying explicit CLI heuristic/order overrides over generated parameter cells. `che_new_autoschedule` also exposes generated preprocessing/search/default schedule lookup with matched-class metadata for C partial-match comments, while `cco_scheduling`'s pure schedule mutation and time/core allocation helpers are represented over owned Rust vectors and the executable schedule bridge uses them for in-process first-cell `--auto-schedule`/`--satauto-schedule` runs. HCB/WFCB default installation, proof-state-driven mutation beyond the represented clause owner, forked multicore strategy scheduling, child-output routing, and final process scheduler installation remain pending until the owner and output routing are fully wired.
- `che_wfcb` evaluation output is now compatible with clause-set eval indexing when clauses are evaluated before insertion. Updating eval indices for clauses that are evaluated after insertion remains a deliberate C-shaped lifecycle hazard to resolve with proof-state ownership.
- `che_learning` executable/proof-state integration remains pending for full learned strategies: full `TBTermParse` parity for the `AnnoTermParse` and `ParseClauseTermRep` paths, shared proof-state term-bank/session ownership, and final strategy installation through heuristic-control callers are not yet available. The Rust `TSMWeight`/`TSMRWeight` WFCBs currently own a private evaluation bank cloned from the compute-time signature and a cloned axiom set, then lazy-load the KB on first compute.
- `ccl_relevance`/`che_funweights` relevance-level weights currently compute relevance from the clause-set side of the axiom context. Formula-set participation and the formula half of `ProofStateRelevancyProcess` remain pending until the staged `WFormula`/`FormulaSet` owner is integrated with proof-state axiom filtering; the executable now uses the clause-side relevance-pruning helper for supported clause-list `--rel-pruning-level` runs.
- `ccl_relevance` inherits two C ordering surfaces that should be revisited only after reference tests pin down pruning output order: `PListStoreP(anchor, ...)` reverses conjecture/rest split lists relative to clause-set order, and `extract_new_core` repeatedly consumes the root of a raw-pointer `PTree` bucket. Rust keeps the reversed PList insertion shape but uses deterministic PList handles for bucket extraction instead of allocator-address-dependent tree roots.
- `che_wfcbadmin` `WeightFunParse`, `WeightFunDefParse`, and `WeightFunDefListParse` dispatch every valid C weight-function parser-table name. Parsers that need `ProofState` data use the Rust axiom-backed parse context for now; invoking those through the no-context wrapper returns an explicit context diagnostic until the full proof-state owner is wired.
- The Rust `che_prio_funs` and `che_wfcb` ports take an explicit `&TermBank` in the evaluation and priority-function paths because current Rust literals do not store the C `eq->bank` back-pointer. Revisit this API once clause/literal ownership has a typed bank handle.
- `PrioFunPreferEasyHO` can return `PrioBest` for a higher-order `DOArgCong` derivation step in C. Rust now emits `DCArgCong` derivation entries for represented ArgCong clauses and the priority helper recognizes that operation code when the process problem type is higher-order.
- Term-ordering options such as `--term-ordering`, `--order-weight-generation`, `--order-precedence-generation`, `--order-constant-weight`, `--precedence`, `--lpo-recursion-limit`, `--literal-comparison`, `--kbo-lam-weight`, and `--kbo-db-weight` are parsed and recorded, including C-compatible executable rejection of `--term-ordering=RPO` even though the shared ordering enum/name table still exposes RPO for strategy-parameter cells and the concrete RPO comparison remains the same downstream unimplemented ordering path, plus C-shaped `--lpo-recursion-limit` large-value warning emission before later option errors. The parsed executable state can now be materialized as a canonical `OrderParmsCell`, and proof-control initialization now selects or creates OCBs through the ported `TOSelectOrdering`/`TOCreateOrdering` path with generated precedence and KBO weights. Remaining ordering work is concrete comparison support for RPO, LFHO/lambda-only comparison branches, and state-owned ordering/index ownership beyond the current proof-control bridge.
- Inference-processing and SAT-check options such as `--sos-uses-input-types`, `--destructive-er`, `--forward-context-sr`, `--prefer-general-demodulators`, `--forward-demod-level`, `--demod-under-lambda`, `--strong-rw-inst`, `--strong-forward-subsumption`, `--lift-lambdas`, `--satcheck`, `--satcheck-proc-interval`, and `--satcheck-decision-limit` are parsed and recorded, with `--strong-forward-subsumption` now honored by proof-control forward subsumption and positive simplify-reflect. The proof-control layer now has a staged `ProcessClause` path plus the outer `Saturate` loop over C-shaped limits, cleanup, timeout, active-watchlist stopping, SAT-check trigger gates, pseudo-ground SATCheck with pure-literal filtering and an internal solver, the C SatCheck-unsat comment on supported executable refutations, C-shaped SAT clause-set insertion limits, DIMACS rendering, non-pure export tracking, and fixed-limit core retrieval helper, presaturation reset/requeue support with caller-owned global-index deletion, and the currently ported first-order plain/simultaneous generators including unindexed paramodulation, first-order-shaped higher-order KBO6 selected-overlap paramodulation, and explicit caller-owned indexed generation/process-clause/saturation variants; supported first-order executable proof search uses configured global PM indexes through that bridge. Higher-order demodulation/subsumption effects, state-owned indexed paramodulation ownership, full LFHO paramodulation surfaces, external PicoSAT integration, and solver-reported SATCheck core extraction still await integration.
- Watchlist, subsumption-indexing, fingerprint-indexing, and PDT constraint options such as `--watchlist`, `--static-watchlist`, `--no-watchlist-simplification`, `--fw-subsumption-aggressive`, `--conventional-subsumption`, `--subsumption-indexing`, `--fvindex-featuretypes`, `--fvindex-maxfeatures`, `--rw-bw-index`, `--pm-from-index`, `--fp-index`, `--pdt-no-size-constr`, and `--pdt-no-age-constr` are parsed and recorded, and the reusable FV-index spec/anchor setup is available. Proof-state watchlist loading, executable watchlist-file activation for supported clause-list input, output-level initial documentation for active watchlist clauses, initial-clause matching, selected/generated-clause local watchlist matching, static-vs-dynamic behavior, and TPTP/TSTP inline watchlist routing from supported normal input/includes are ported; final state-owned watchlist global-index attachment, fingerprint index allocation, and PDT constraint effects still await proof-state and indexing integration.
- Reporting, filtering, strategy, and limit options such as `--print-statistics`, `--print-saturated`, `--filter-saturated`, `--select-strategy`, `--parse-strategy`, `--print-strategy`, `--processed-clauses-limit`, and `--answers` are parsed and recorded. `--print-saturated` now emits C's success-clause prelude for proof-found outcomes plus descriptor-selected proof-state sections after supported clause-list saturation, and the prelude/sections honor the parsed output/equation-print options plus full-term `--print-types` suffixes where the ported literal printers support them. `--filter-saturated` reaches the final unprocessed-set filter bridge for configured runs and can promote filtered empty clauses to proof-found results, while the CLI still preserves C's descriptor-validator mismatch that admits only no-op filter letters. `--print-statistics`/output levels above 1 emit the maintained proof-state counters plus executable-level clause-list counts with the reference `%` comment prefix, document parsed initial clauses and active watchlist clauses through the shared C `ClauseSetDocInital`/`DocClauseCreation(inf_initial)` PCL/TSTP creation session before proof-control initialization, document proof-success clauses through the C `DocClauseQuote(..., "proof")` PCL/TSTP shapes before the proof banner, and document stopped final proof-state clauses through the C `ProofStatePropDocQuote` PCL/TSTP shapes before non-proof result banners; `--proof-object=1` also emits represented proof-object list framing and clause-side derived/source payloads for supported proof-found, complete saturated no-proof, and forced stopped clause-list runs, with lists expanded to represented ancestors before extraction roots and represented `--full-deriv` processed/unprocessed roots when requested. `--parse-strategy` and `--select-strategy` feed `HeuristicParmsCell` before proof-control initialization, `--print-strategy` covers the current/named/all predefined strategy surfaces, supported `--auto` proof-search/CNF runs emit C-shaped preprocessing/search class and generated configuration comments, supported `--auto-schedule`/`--satauto-schedule` first-order runs emit preprocessing/search class lines plus C-shaped schedule summaries before running the selected in-process strategy, and supported proof-search/CNF exits emit C-shaped result banners plus `PRINT_TSTP_STATUS` SZS status lines for proof found, answer tuples, presaturation interreduction, saturated/no proof, restricted-calculus closure, incomplete out-of-unprocessed, empty watchlist, user resource exhaustion, and CNF-only success. CNF-only output is also covered for the supported temporary formula bridge after formula lowering. Forked multicore process scheduling, full ordered proof-object extraction/renumbering, formula/preprocessing-aware reporting, and subsystem-global statistics counters still await parser, heuristic, and saturation integration.
- `--syntax-only`, `--print-formulas`, `--prune`, `--cnf`, and `--error-on-empty` are available for the current clause-parser subset and the supported temporary formula bridge after lowering. `--syntax-only` now emits the C success banner and `Unknown` SZS status when parsing succeeds without formula printing, `--print-formulas` renders supported parsed clauses through LOP, old-TPTP, or TSTP output according to the parsed output format, follows selected TSTP includes before rendering, the LOP branch consumes the parsed equation-print options, and full-term clause output consumes `--print-types`; with automatic input format detection, TSTP clause input now flips the runtime output format to TSTP as in C. `--prune` now emits the C pruning-success banner after supported clause-list or formula-bridge parsing, applies clause-side `--rel-pruning-level`, threshold SInE, clause-side GSinE, and represented `LambdaDef` before initial docs, prints the selected SInE filter line for resolved filters, and uses the same auto-detected TSTP documentation-format side effect for initial clause docs. `--cnf` prints the initialized lowered clause state for supported formula-bridge input without entering saturation. Full formula-owner printing, formula-aware destructive SInE pruning, formula relevance pruning, formula CNF helper call-site integration, and complete parser/preprocessing integration remain pending.
- `TCPReadTextBlock` has both iterator-backed tests and a network-backed helper, but executable call sites are not yet wired through real sockets.
- `GlobalOutFD` is exact for stdout, Unix file targets, and native MSVC Windows file targets. The Windows path duplicates the owned Rust file handle into a C-runtime file descriptor behind a narrowly scoped external-DLL boundary so the descriptor has independent close ownership; non-MSVC Windows toolchains still use an explicit unknown descriptor sentinel until a matching CRT abstraction is chosen.
- `TCPChannelAlloc`/`TCPChannelClose` are represented with owned `Read + Write` streams instead of raw integer socket descriptors; this preserves queue and transfer behavior for Rust callers but defers exact descriptor lifetime, `close(2)` diagnostics, and `select`/`fd_set` integration until server control code is ported.
- `CreateServerSock`/`Listen`/`CreateClientSock` are represented by safe `TcpListener`/`TcpStream` wrappers rather than raw integer socket descriptors; Linux server-socket creation now sets `SO_REUSEADDR`, binds, and applies the C backlog before wrapping the listening socket, while non-Linux reuse semantics, descriptor-number identity, close diagnostics, and exact `gai_strerror` diagnostics still need platform-specific abstractions if server/client programs require byte-identical behavior.
- `ESignalSetup`/`ESignalHandler` preserve signal-state transitions as explicit Rust outcomes and Linux normal builds now install a `SIGXCPU` trampoline that latches the soft-timeout path and performs the C-shaped reset/rearm `RLIMIT_CPU` sequence for soft timeouts; test builds keep the handler non-mutating. Hard CPU-limit outcomes now have a non-signal finalizer for the C doubled-`COMCHAR` `ResourceOut` banner/SZS output, stderr diagnostic, CPU-limit exit status, and supported stdout buffering order, and executable proof search maps cooperative hard-deadline stops through that finalizer. Direct descriptor writes from the handler, process `exit`, and SIGTERM default-handler restoration/re-raise still require a tighter platform shutdown abstraction.
- `TempFileName` now uses libc `mkstemp` on Linux, matching the C suffix replacement and file-mode source more closely, while non-Linux targets still use a safe `create_new` retry loop that preserves directory choice, prefix, registration, and empty-file creation but not exact libc suffix distribution or permissions.
- Permanent strings are represented as `Arc<str>` rather than raw `char*`; duplicate calls preserve shared allocation identity, while clearing the registry does not invalidate existing Rust handles.
- The JKISS wrapper preserves the exported C module's static-state behavior, including the fact that the `JKISSRand` state argument does not drive the random sequence; the initial random-weight evaluator calls through that wrapper, but full heuristic-control integration still needs reference coverage for random-dependent strategies.
- The executable option table now covers the C `e_options.h` long-option, short-alias, argument-kind, and default-argument surface, with regression tests comparing Rust option metadata against the C `opts[]` table. Help text remains intentionally concise rather than byte-compatible with the C prose.
- Running the Rust binary on supported first-order clause-list input now enters the ported proof-control initialization and saturation path, including configured watchlist-file loading/inline activation before proof initialization with output-level initial documentation after parsed clause docs, TPTP/TSTP inline watchlist routing from supported normal input/includes, OutputLevel 1 dynamic watchlist reduction text during initialization and saturation, OutputLevel 1 selected-clause progress text during saturation, predefined strategy selection/parsing into proof-control parameters, generated `--auto` preprocessing/search strategy selection, in-process first-cell generated `--auto-schedule`/`--satauto-schedule` selection with C-shaped schedule summaries and schedule-cell ordering preload, optional `--presat-simplify` no-generation presaturation interreduction, C-shaped proof-search result banners and SZS status lines using the reference `%` `COMCHAR`, output-level proof-success and stopped-final documentation quotes, represented proof-object list output for `--proof-object=1` on proof-found, complete saturated no-proof, and forced stopped exits with available clause-side derivation/source payloads, represented ancestor expansion, and represented proof-success `--full-deriv` roots, answer tuple output for explicit and supported question-derived answer literals, restricted-calculus and incomplete out-of-unprocessed final statuses, descriptor-selected saturated-state output including the proof-success clause prelude and `--print-sat-info` same-line `info(...)` comments, the final saturated-filter bridge, maintained statistics output when requested, and the preprocessing-time line plus resource-usage footer when `--resources-info` is selected; `--syntax-only` and `--prune` parse supported clause files without entering proof search, and `--print-strategy` exits early after strategy output. Full formula parsing/preprocessing, forked multicore/process strategy scheduling, full ordered proof-object extraction/renumbering, state-owned global indexes, and higher-order proof search remain pending.
- `FuncSymbParse`, `TermParseOperator`, term-symbol signature insertion, unshared term parsing, and simple term-bank parsing are present for string scanners, including list literals for list-support signatures, checked `$ite` term parsing through the temporary formula subset, non-Boolean checked term-bank argument handling, non-Boolean `$ite` term branches over fixed non-predicate atoms and compound applications, scoped fixed non-Boolean `$let` definitions and bodies, public `TFormulaTPTPParse`- and `TFormulaTSTPParse`-shaped parsers for truth constants, atoms, equality/disequality, negation, first-order quantifiers, old-TPTP right-recursive binary operators, named lambda formula operands, typed higher-order formula application with `@` including parenthesized logical heads, `$ite`, scoped Boolean `$let`, and FOF binary/associative connectives, a `TSTPDistinctParse`-shaped pseudo-formula parser that allocates same-type zero-arity constants directly, and a clause-layer `TcfTSTPParse` wrapper for unquantified `|`-connected clause bodies plus C-shaped universal prefixes whose parenthesized bodies stay on the clause parser and whose comma-separated variables become recursive one-variable quantifier nodes. Non-fixed term-valued FOOL atom ambiguity, full formula-owner integration, and scanner file/include handling remain incomplete.
- `ccl_clauses` full `TBTermParse` syntax parity, full formula-owner-aware TSTP rendering, the global `ClausePrint` output-format dispatcher, clause-set eval-index object back-pointers, clause-set linkage, HO definition extraction, and full eta-normalizing choice recognition are still deferred until formula printing/parsing, ordering control, clause-set handle ownership, lambda/HO-CSU, and stable choice-clause handles are available. Simple-term-backed clause parsing, represented choice-axiom recognition and choice instantiation, skolemized copying, OCB-backed maximal-term marking and multiset greater/equal rejection, first-order LOP/TPTP/PCL/TSTP-core string helpers, typed first-order TSTP clause formula output, and represented higher-order THF clause formula output are available for callers that can pass an explicit term bank and format options.
- `ccl_neweval` splay-tree operations remain available with explicit Rust arena handles for callers that need C-shaped tree behavior, while `ClauseSet` now owns safe sorted eval roots for clause-owned evaluation cells.
- `ccl_clausesets` ClauseSet-owned demodulator/PDT index lifecycle integration, full `TBTermParse` parser parity, full global-output/documentation routines, formula/list split helpers, and derivation statistics are deferred until the corresponding owners, printers, indexes, and higher-order term-ordering helpers are ported; pointer-identity `ClauseSetFind`, borrowed-clause demodulator verification, explicit-anchor and owned FV-indexed insertion/indexification, simple-term-backed parsing, OCB-backed maximal-term marking, explicit LOP/TPTP/TSTP set and prefix printing, and explicit LOP/TPTP equality axiom printing are available.
- `ccl_formulasets` has an initial `WFormula`/`FormulaSet` owner surface with C-shaped allocation defaults, generated id rendering, property/type helpers, append-order insertion, set-to-set movement, extraction/deletion by stable entry id, untyped/interpreted scans, lambda-definition-excluding standard weight, conjecture splitting/counting/order, wrapper f-code return/symbol-diversity/lambda-defined-symbol/conversion/non-app-printing/app-encode/simplify/question-conjecture preprocessing/FOOL-unroll/definition-introduction/CNF2 helpers, formula-set printing and app encoding, staged `FormulaSetSimplify` with optional thresholded term GC, `FormulaSetPreprocConjectures`, `WFormulaSetUnrollFOOL`, `TFormulaSetIntroduceDefs`, `TFormulaSetDelTermpProp`, `TFormulaSetNamedToDBLambdas`, `TFormulaSetLiftItes`, `TFormulaSetLiftLets`, `TFormulaSetLambdaNormalize`, `FormulaSetArchive`, `FormulaSetDocInital`, and `FormulaSetCNF2` supported phase pipeline with higher-order named-to-DB/ITE/LET/lambda-to-forall preprocessing and thresholded CNF-path term GC, definition-statistics aggregation, stack cardinality, conditional stack type propagation, formula-term GC marking through `tb_gc_collect`, formula-set polarity marking, and proof-state `f_axioms`/`f_ax_archive`/`f_archive` ownership for counting, `$distinct` expansion, reset, statistics, and represented term-bank GC marking. Parser/remaining formula-set CNF preprocessing/executable formula call-site integration and exact pointer-stable formula handles remain pending.
- `ccl_clausefunc` exact orphan deletion over stable proof-state-owned parent handles, proof-documentation side effects, and full proof-control injectivity ownership integration are still deferred until formula wrappers, stable proof-state handles, full printing, and caller ownership are ported; explicit LOP/TPTP/TSTP stack rendering, archive move/copy helpers with `DCCnfQuote` derivation metadata, injected compact-parent orphan checks/deletion, proof-control source-aware compact-parent snapshots, plain injectivity-definition recognition/replacement, naked Boolean-variable normalization derivation metadata, and term-formula clause collection/CNF insertion are available.
- `ccl_condensation` `DCCondense` derivation metadata is ported, and an opt-in documenting helper now emits represented condensation proof-documentation before the derivation push for proof-control callers with a `ProofDocSession`.
- `ccl_eqn` full `TBTermParse` syntax parity and formula-level TSTP integration are still deferred until the full parsing and printing layers are available; simple-term-backed parsing plus OCB-backed equation orientation/comparison, split-mod standard-weight gating with caller-supplied special-symbol weights, term-extension literal weights, and explicit app-encoded, debug, dereferenced, FOF, and TSTP literal rendering are available.
- `ccl_eqnresolution` full higher-order CSU enumeration and `subst_is_ho` propagation to higher-order derivation flags remain pending; first-order single and all-resolvent paths, their higher-order-problem first-order subset, DB-lambda beta/eta normalization of copied resolvents, derivation entries, opt-in all-resolvent creation proof-documentation output, and opt-in destructive equality-resolution modification proof-documentation output are available.
- `ccl_eqnlist` full `TBTermParse` syntax parity and exact no-copy pointer-stack conversions are still deferred until full parsing and stable literal-handle ownership are available; simple-term-backed parsing, C `EqnMap`-style term mapping, DB-lambda beta/eta list normalization, OCB-backed list orientation/maximal marking, and explicit dereferenced/TSTP list rendering are available.
- `ccl_f_generality` has clause distribution, staged formula distribution including C `TermTrimImplications`, clause/formula D-relation selection, and distribution debug rendering available.
- `ccl_sine` executable formula-aware `SelectAxioms` and pointer-owner-discovering mixed movement remain deferred until parser/preprocessing formulas are connected to stable executable clause/formula handles; clause/formula D-relation construction including trimmed conjecture formula D-relations, queue seed discovery for clauses and formulas, staged mixed GSinE selection traversal, threshold selection including staged formula result stacks, exact staged `SelectDefinitions`, destructive threshold and clause-side GSinE integration for represented proof-state clause axioms, represented executable `LambdaDef`, debug rendering, TSTP clause/formula-stack rendering, stack-wide clause/formula property deletion, and staged clause/formula stack movement are available.
- `ccl_fcvindexing` exact `IntMap` density/storage accounting and exact mixed-stream tree output are deferred until index profiling and output routing are available; explicit LOP/TPTP/TSTP tree rendering is available for callers that pass a term bank, output format, and problem type.
- `ccl_freqvectors` raw `FreqVectorCell`/`FVPackedClause` clause aliasing is deferred until stable clause ownership is available; explicit optional-clause LOP/TPTP/TSTP vector rendering is available without raw aliasing.
- `ccl_grounding` process-global `GroundSetPrintDimacs` output routing, direct process exit for estimated instance limits, and executable integration of constrained grounding are still deferred until full ground-term ownership and output/proof-state integration are ported; explicit `GroundSetPrint`-style output dispatch, C-compatible DIMACS split-writer helpers, and explicit single-clause and clause-set grounding progress output are available.
- `ccl_propclauses` process-global `ClausePrint` hidden-state routing is intentionally represented as explicit LOP/TPTP/TSTP output-format dispatch; executable integration still needs to thread the selected format into every caller that printed through C globals.
- `cpr_varset` uses one raw pointer type for both the atom-set sentinel and extractable list cells. Rust exposes separate owner and cell-handle types, but still preserves front insertion, arbitrary extraction, duplicates, and invalid `PLiteralNoLit` extraction as an invariant failure.
- `cpr_propsig` lookup is backed by safe owned strings and a deterministic Rust map rather than C's `StrTree` node storage. This preserves name/code behavior and print order, but does not preserve lookup-time tree reorganization or shared raw `char*` identity between the stack and tree.
- `cpr_propclauses` conversion is ported with an explicit `TermBank` parameter rather than C's `eqn->bank` back-pointer, and normalization follows the documented absolute-code ordering rather than the apparent `p_atom_compare` typo that makes the C `qsort` comparator non-strict.
- `cpr_dpllformula` parsing is exposed with an explicit Rust `TermBank` rather than C's borrowed-signature temporary bank, and active-clause indexes store clause indices rather than raw pointers. Formula printing preserves the C atom-debug typo that prints positive occurrence counts in both columns.
- `cpr_dpll` currently ports only the implemented state shell. The C `deactivate_clauses` and `shorten_clauses` helpers are empty stubs, `DPLLRetractLastAss` is declared but has no definition in `cpr_dpll.c`, and Rust does not invent the missing propagation/backtracking behavior in this slice.
- `pcl_expressions` ports expression-tree parsing/printing and is used by the current full-step, mini-step, protocol, proofcheck, and lemma-analysis surfaces. It preserves the C expression-position parser mismatch and TSTP position omission.
- `pcl_idents` stores live components in a Rust `Vec<i64>` rather than a sentinel-terminated `PDArray`, while preserving the sentinel at comparison boundaries used by the current full-step and protocol surfaces.
- `pcl_miniclauses` stores safe shared `Term` handles rather than borrowed raw `Term_p` arrays, uses `usize` rather than C's truncating `short literal_no`, preserves C's clause-property loss, and follows the C temporary-clause printing path used by `MiniClausePrint` and the current mini-step/protocol surfaces. Its Rust `MiniClausePrint` equivalent makes C's hidden output-format/problem-type globals explicit. Direct compact printer optimization remains deferred.
- `pcl_miniprotocol` stores steps in a safe `Vec<Option<PclMiniStep>>` rather than a raw-pointer `PDArray`, rejects duplicate ids by id rather than asserting same-pointer reinsertion, exposes scanner-comment forwarding through explicit output-aware parsing, and preserves mini-protocol quirks including positive-id-only protocol parsing, no-separator protocol printing, contiguous-suffix fast proof marking, and deterministic id-based precondition collection instead of pointer-tree ordering.
- `pcl_ministeps` uses an explicit shell-parse option rather than C's process-global `SupportShellPCL`, requires the caller-owned term bank for formula/clause rendering instead of storing `TB_p`, and preserves mini-protocol quirks including numeric-only ids, SQ-string-only extras, id-zero parse/free mismatch, and shell TSTP double-comma output.
- `pcl_positions` stores term paths in a Rust `Vec<i64>` rather than C's nullable `PDArray`, and preserves the legacy printer shape that omits dots before term-position components. Broader PCL2 protocol integration remains deferred.
- `pcl_protocol` stores steps in a sorted `Vec<PclStep>` rather than a raw-pointer `PTree` with cached `PStack`, rejects duplicates without reproducing C's pre-error count increment, exposes scanner-comment forwarding through explicit output-aware parsing, reports dangling precondition references as syntax diagnostics rather than allowing null parent pointers, preserves FOF stripping's justification-only reset without setting `PCLIsInitial`, and uses deterministic PCL-id precondition ordering instead of pointer-tree ordering.
- `pcl_steps` stores clausal logical content behind a Rust `Box<Clause>` rather than a raw union arm, requires a caller-owned term bank for formula/clause rendering instead of storing `TB_p`, and uses explicit shell-parse options rather than C's process-global `SupportShellPCL`. It preserves the stale `PCLType1` comment mismatch as documentation only, the `que` parser diagnostic omission, the `PCLTypeAxiom` versus `PCLIsInitial` TSTP-role distinction, full-step extras accepting `Name|PosInt` beyond mini-step `SQString`, formula-backed TPTP output without a final period, and shell logical-format omission markers plus warning side-channel wrappers.
- `ccl_subsumption` ClauseSet-owned FV-index lifecycle integration, indexed simplify-reflect lookup, and PCL/documentation side effects are still deferred until clause sets, FV indexes, unit simplification indexes, documentation, and proof-state ownership are available. The strong-unit forward-subsumption option now reaches proof-control forward subsumption and positive simplify-reflect through session state rather than a C-style process global.
- Simple types are represented with safe shared handles rather than raw `Type_p`; this preserves pointer identity for Rust-held values, but allocator address reuse and exact pointer ordering may differ from C.
- Term cells are represented with safe shared handles and interior mutability rather than raw flexible-array `TermCell` allocations. Ordinary variable binding dereference, global no-cache applied-variable `TermDeref` expansion, scoped bank-local no-WHNF applied-variable expansion, the DB-lambda `ApplyTerms`/`FlattenApps`/`UnfoldLambda`/`drop_args`/`find_min_db`/`LambdaEtaExpandDBTopLevel`/`LambdaEtaExpandDB`/`LambdaEtaReduceDB`/`LambdaNormalizeDB`/`CloseWithDBVar`/`CloseWithTypePrefix`/`ShiftDB`/`WHNF_step`/explicit-bank `WHNF_deref`/beta-normalization subset needed by pruning, comparison-local LFHO KBO6 weak-head dereferencing for `DEREF_ALWAYS`, and the `MakeRewrittenTerm` retained-argument construction used by `TBTermPosReplace` are present, but WHNF cache behavior, binding caches, owner-bank metadata, and full LFHO bank insertion remain deferred until the complete lambda-normalization path is ported.
- `TermPosDebugPrint` supports both C modes for currently ported term surfaces: null-signature address output and term-bank-backed non-null-signature output that prints each superterm as `DEREF_NEVER...DEREF_ALWAYS` before the child index. Full FOOL formula, `let`, and lambda pretty-printing still needs formula-printer integration.
- `TermFree`/`TermTopFree` ownership transfer is not exposed yet because Rust term handles are reference-counted; future term-bank integration must define when unshared term trees are intentionally consumed versus borrowed.
- Variable banks currently keep the default type handle from construction rather than a live mutable pointer to the whole `TypeBank`; parser integration should ensure variable banks are built after the shared type bank has the required default and user sorts.

## C Behaviors To Revisit After Compatibility

These notes are not permission to diverge during porting. They identify inherited C behaviors that may be good cleanup candidates after the Rust executable is demonstrably drop-in compatible.

- `DStr`, `PStack`, `DStack`, `PDArray`, `DDArray`, and `PDRangeArr` preserve C growth and mutating-access patterns; later APIs may want clearer separation between read-only access and access that allocates or extends storage.
- `clb_avlgeneric` is an obsolete name for generic binary-search-tree traversal; Rust keeps that naming only at the module boundary and uses handle-based traversal internally.
- `PStack`/`DStack` discard helpers intentionally use swap-remove behavior, which is efficient but order-destroying; keep auditing callers before exposing order-preserving variants.
- `ProofStateAnalyseGC` accumulates into `gc_count` and `gc_used_count` on every call instead of resetting or returning a pure report. Rust preserves this for compatibility, but later reporting APIs should make cumulative versus one-shot GC analysis explicit.
- `PList` raw-pointer anchors and extracted-cell ownership are represented with checked arena handles plus freed-slot reuse to match allocator-shaped reuse. Stale handles can become valid for new cells; after list-heavy clause/formula code is ported, revisit whether a generational freelist or typed owner should replace this compatibility shape.
- `PLocalStack` intentionally keeps the C split between `ensure_space` and push; later term traversal code may want scoped helper APIs that make missing ensures impossible without losing the C growth profile.
- `PQueue` exposes absolute internal ring indices for iteration and C has a public grow routine that is only coherent for the full-ring state; later callers should keep those details encapsulated behind safer traversal APIs.
- `FixedDArray` currently mirrors C assertion-style size contracts; callers fed by user input may eventually need recoverable error paths instead of invariant panics.
- `MinHeap` preserves the C helper directions where `decr_key` drops down and `incr_key` bubbles up, despite those names appearing reversed for a conventional min-heap. Rename or wrap only after all heap users are audited.
- `ObjTree` and `ObjMap` replace comparison-function/raw-pointer storage with ordered Rust keys and explicit ownership; revisit this once term, clause, and substitution arenas define stable handle identity and deleter responsibilities.
- `ClausePositiveSimplifyReflect` and `ClauseNegativeSimplifyReflect` are coupled to demodulator indexes and proof-documentation side effects in C. The Rust plain-set path keeps the literal-removal behavior linear while recording `DCSR` and exposes opt-in proof-doc wrappers through explicit sessions; revisit the indexed lookup and stable-parent coupling once indexes and proof documentation are owned by proof state.
- `ClauseInfoPrint` labels field `d6` as variable occurrences, but computes it through `ClauseWeight(..., max_term_multiplier=0, vweight=1, fweight=1, ...)`, including C's equality-predicate correction and orientation/maximality effects. Rust preserves the printed value; a later diagnostic-oriented API should either rename the field or compute direct variable occurrences.
- `StrongUnitForwardSubsumption` is process-global in C and changes positive-unit simplify behavior. Rust routes configured proof search through a `ProofControl` session flag plus explicit helper parameters; revisit only if later strategy scheduling needs C-global sharing semantics.
- `ClauseClauseSubsumptionCalls`, `ClauseClauseSubsumptionCallsRec`, `ClauseClauseSubsumptionSuccesses`, and `UnitClauseClauseSubsumptionCalls` are process-global mutable counters in C. Rust uses atomics for compatibility-shaped observation; move these into explicit proof/session statistics once the prover state owns saturation.
- `ClauseSetFindSubsumedClause` and `ClauseSetFindUnitSubsumedClause` accept a raw `Clause_p set_position` inside the set's linked list. Rust temporarily models this as a start index over the plain owner; replace it with stable clause handles when proof-state clause ownership is ported.
- FV-indexed subsumption in C finds leaf candidates through `PTree` root/left/right traversal and raw clause-pointer identity. Rust's current `FvIndex` searches cloned clause snapshots in deterministic identifier-keyed `BTreeMap` order so safe value moves can still delete indexed payloads; replace this with stable clause handles before treating indexed search order as proof-state-visible.
- `ClauseSet` owns the `fvindex` pointer in C and query wrappers silently branch on that embedded pointer. Rust now has an owned optional FV anchor and keeps explicit-anchor wrappers for transition tests; stable clause handles are still needed before long-lived FV-index payloads can stop cloning clause snapshots or depending on clause identifiers as their deletion key.
- `ccl_neweval` comparison treats equal `eval_count` values as fully equal before looking at heuristic values, and otherwise compares heuristic before falling back to FIFO count. Rust preserves that branch order; later typed evaluation handles should make duplicate counts impossible outside deliberate C-shaped imports.
- `ccl_neweval` stores heuristic values as `float` even though WFCB evaluation functions return `double`, so assignment truncates precision before ordering. Rust stores `f32` in `SimpleEvalCell`; revisit this only after reference tests establish whether higher precision changes clause selection.
- `ccl_neweval` permits freeing evaluation cells without checking whether a tree still references them. Rust exposes the same detached-node/freeing shape through handle removal, but later clause-set owners should prefer ownership APIs that prevent dangling evaluation handles.
- Low-level OS-facing wrappers use POSIX `getrlimit`/`setrlimit`, `clock`, `sysconf`, `gettimeofday`, `getrusage`, `strerror`, and close-style APIs in C; Rust uses scoped unsafe external-library boundaries for Linux `getrlimit`/`setrlimit`, `strerror`, `clock`, `sysconf`, `signal`, Unix `close`, resource-footer `getrusage`, and Windows `Kernel32`/`CloseHandle` process timing, page size, physical memory, peak working-set reporting, input-close ownership, and Job Object memory limiting, and keeps safe Linux `/proc` parsing only as a fallback. Resource-limit APIs outside Linux and Windows still return explicit unsupported results. Keep future unsafe code similarly confined to external DLL/shared-library interop with documented invariants.
- `StrTree`, `FloatTree`, `NumTree`, `NumXTree`, `PTree`, and `QuadTree` model ordered semantics with safe Rust containers while documenting splay-locality gaps; hot indexing paths should be benchmarked before deciding whether to recreate splay behavior.
- `RegMem` in C stores raw allocation pointers in a global `PTree` and frees them at process shutdown; the Rust handle registry preserves cleanup semantics, but later code should prefer explicit owner structs where persistent scratch memory can be scoped.
- `IntMap` keeps C density transitions and null-slot quirks, but its hidden read/delete-time array growth in C is a likely cleanup target if compatibility tests show no observable dependency.
- `ClauseIsTautologyReal` uses a fixed `MAX_EQ_TAUTOLOGY_CHECK_LITNO` cutoff of 1000 negative literals and falls back to only trivial tautology detection beyond that point; after compatibility is secured, this cost/coverage tradeoff should be revisited against real benchmark clauses.
- `ccl_tautologies` computes ground normal forms by repeatedly copying unshared terms, structurally matching complete rule sides, and reinserting changed sides into the term bank; this is intentionally simple C behavior, but a future rewrite-indexed or term-bank-aware normalizer may be worth profiling.
- `ClauseIsTautologyReal(..., copy=false)` in C mutates and then frees the caller-provided clause pointer; the Rust API keeps caller ownership explicit by using a shallow work clone for the no-copy term path.
- `clb_memory` and `clb_newmem` rely on exact-size frees, debug poisoning words, and raw reuse of uninitialized blocks; Rust keeps initialized buffers and explicit policies, so term/clause arena work should decide whether a specialized typed allocator is needed for comparable performance.
- Permanent strings use `Arc<str>` to keep Rust handles valid after registry clearing; later term/signature ownership should decide whether stable arena handles are a better identity model.
- `AtomSetFree` in `cpr_varset` drains a circular list cell-by-cell, and the public API permits `PLiteralNoLit` insertion even though extraction asserts on that payload. Rust keeps this behavior for compatibility; revisit a typed nonzero literal wrapper or contiguous worklist representation after DPLL caller behavior and performance are measured.
- `PropSigAtomNumber` in `cpr_propsig` exposes the raw stack pointer including the reserved null slot, while `PropSigGetAtomEnc` has lookup-time `StrTreeFind` reorganization and `PropSigInsertAtom` stores one raw name pointer in two containers. Rust keeps the public count and behavior but uses safe string ownership; later APIs should separate usable atom count, lookup-performance policy, and name interning explicitly.
- `p_atom_compare` in `cpr_propclauses` appears to compare `ABS(*a1)` to itself, making sort order depend on library `qsort` behavior instead of the documented atom-code order. Rust uses the documented order for a deterministic DPLL base; revisit this only with reference-output tests if legacy DPLL behavior turns out to depend on the typo.
- `DPLLRegisterClauseLiteral` in `cpr_dpllformula` grows atom storage using the signed literal while indexing by `ABS(lit)`, and `DPLLFormulaPrint` prints `pos_occur` twice instead of the negative occurrence count. Rust grows by absolute atom code to avoid a memory hazard and preserves the print typo only in legacy rendering; revisit both once the DPLL path has reference tests.
- `cpr_dpll` has no real propagation today: `deactivate_clauses` and `shorten_clauses` return zero, `DPLLAssignVar` therefore reports failure for every allocated assignment, and the negative-assignment branch still touches the same positive/negative active sets as the positive branch after negating the assignment. Real DPLL work should replace these stubs with reference-tested propagation and add the missing retraction implementation deliberately.
- `PCLExprParse` in `pcl_expressions` checks for an opening parenthesis before calling `PCL2PosParse`, which then expects a positive integer and fails without consuming the parenthesis; `PCLExprPrintTSTP` also ignores stored positions. Rust preserves both surfaces until reference PCL traces clarify whether this is dead code or a compatibility bug.
- `PCLIdParse` in `pcl_idents` parses fullstop-separated identifier components despite the source comment saying the list is separated by spaces, and `PCLIdCompare` subtracts `long` components before truncating to `int`. Rust preserves those compatibility surfaces in the current helper, but cleaned proof-object APIs should use explicit syntax comments and lexicographic ordering.
- `PCL2PosPrint` in `pcl_positions` omits dots before term-position components even though `PCL2PosParse` requires dotted components, and the header's optional-literal wording does not match the parser's mandatory leading positive integer. Rust preserves the output behavior and parser contract until PCL2 trace compatibility is established.
- `clb_simple_stuff` includes historical global-state behavior in `ProblemType` and JKISS random generation; Rust currently resets `ProblemType` around executable `run()` calls while preserving C-shaped first-order state during parsing and solving, but these states should move into an explicit proof-session context before parallel parsing or solving is introduced.
- Error handling is currently represented with structured `Diagnostic` values in many Rust paths; final executable compatibility still needs exact fatal-error stream, wording, and exit-status behavior.
- Partial-ordering symbol rendering should be audited once ordering output is wired in, because the C table intentionally omits printable strings for cache-only comparison results.
- `SysDateInc` mutates the date before its assertion, so incrementing the invalid sentinel produces the creation date and then fails; Rust reports that state explicitly. The C signed-overflow case is undefined, while Rust reports overflow without wrapping.
- `SysDatePrint` prints signed `SysDate` with `%5lu`, effectively treating negative dates as unsigned `long` bit patterns on the reference platform. Keep this only for compatibility-sensitive rendering.
- `clb_verbose` relies on process-global mutable verbosity and direct `stderr` side effects. Rust currently centralizes this behind atomic state and writer-parameterized helpers; later session code should decide whether verbosity belongs in explicit run/session state.
- `eprover.c` routes nearly all ordinary output through process-global `GlobalOut` and separately caches `fileno(GlobalOut)` for low-memory/signal descriptor writes. Rust keeps the initial run wrapper test-capturable for stdout, opens configured files locally, and mirrors the descriptor side channel where the platform can do so with safe ownership; once the full parser/prover pipeline owns output, decide whether this descriptor cache remains compatibility-only or becomes explicit session state.
- `eprover.c` drives syntax-only parsing through the normal proof-state scanner and formula/clause parser pipeline. The Rust executable currently uses a standalone `TermBank`/`ClauseSet` and the available simple clause-list parser for `--syntax-only`, though it now applies the same configured distinct-symbol mask used by proof-state allocation; rejoin this with full proof-state/formula/list/`let`/`ite` parsing once those owners are ported.
- C handles old TPTP `input_formula(...)` and TSTP `fof(...)`/`tff(...)`/`tcf(...)` parsing through term-encoded `TFormula` owners and the CNF pipeline before clauses enter proof state. Rust's executable-level formula bridge is deliberately narrower and currently performs C-shaped wrapper-role validation, old-TPTP formula role defaulting, old-TPTP same-precedence right-recursive binary operator parsing with quantifiers scoped to the next elementary formula unless parenthesized, old-TPTP FOOL-primary existential-body parsing through that elementary-formula path, lowered `question` role preservation, top-level THF type-declaration signature mutation, simple first-order-shaped and predicate-application THF formula lowering, typed THF quantified variable declarations, Positive universal and nested existential scopes under a positive existential are bridged with an explicit active-universal stack (TSTP rejects globally free variables before lowering, while old TPTP bridge entries still seed that stack from globally free formula variables), filtered to variables that occur in the existential body, and grouped conjunction/disjunction/existential, negated-universal/existential, implication/equivalence/NAND/NOR operands now reuse that polarity-aware lowering. The bridge also preserves supported connective variants through parsing so app-encoded formula output can keep C's source spellings before semantic clause lowering. The bridge mirrors C's mutable scoped external-name environment only for supported quantifier scopes, and type declarations skip C's temporary `$true` formula placeholder until formula-set ownership exists. - The temporary THF application bridge recovers declared application heads when the executable's first-order global problem-type state would create `_ARITYFIX0` placeholders for `p @ a`. Replace that compatibility shim by threading the wrapper/problem type through term parsing when the full formula owner/parser path lands.
- Formula-level Boolean `$ite` and `$let` currently reach Rust's temporary bridge as already typed Boolean terms. Direct positive Boolean `$ite(C,T,F)` predicate literals now lower through the C `do_ite_unroll` formula-position shape `(~C|T)&(C|F)`, and direct literals containing a term-position `$ite` now lower the first occurrence on the left side before the right side into `(~C|L[T])&(C|L[F])`, matching C's direct literal-side scan for the supported bridge. Direct `$let` occurrences through supported quantifier/connective scopes now lift before CNF by allocating fresh `epred`/`esk` definitions over captured and formal variables, replacing body occurrences, and clausifying Boolean definitions as equivalences or term definitions as equalities with free variables in generated definition clauses carrying the expected universal clause meaning; conjecture lowering keeps those generated definitions positive while negating only the rewritten conjecture body. App-encode/syntax-only still keep the original term encoding. The simple formula parser has a special primary-formula branch, reused by unparenthesized existential-body parsing, so equality and disequality around Boolean FOOL atoms are represented as equivalence and XOR instead of term equality, while non-Boolean `$ite`/`$let` primary terms remain valid only when immediately followed by `=` or `!=` and then lower as ordinary term literals. A full formula owner should model these as typed formula/term nodes, with explicit predicate-literal, `ite`/`let`, and term-equality lowering steps instead of depending on predicate-position checks to recognize them.
- The C formula owner treats `$let` definition-head variables as scoped formals while later lifting collects formal and captured variables into generated definitions. Rust now mirrors the supported direct `$let` surface in the temporary bridge's free-variable and dependency scans, including conjecture bodies, and keeps generated definition clauses at default/plain metadata without input source annotations. The full owner should still make let-local formal scope, captured-variable dependencies, generated-definition derivation/archive metadata, and broader owner-side lifting explicit instead of inferring them from term-encoded cells.
- The temporary `--app-encode` bridge renders internal Boolean `$let` cells back to a TPTP-style `$let(...)` while recursively app-encoding formula-valued definitions and bodies. Because let-local symbols still live in the shared temporary signature, their symbol declarations can leak into the top-level app-encoded declaration prelude; a full `FormulaSet` owner should make let-local declaration scope explicit before deciding whether to preserve that leakage for compatibility.
- Old TPTP `TFormulaTPTPParse` has no usual precedence ladder for binary formula operators and gives quantifiers only an elementary body by default. Rust preserves that for `input_formula(...)`; a later cleaned parser should keep dialect-specific precedence and quantifier scope visible in the API instead of sharing one generic FOF helper across old TPTP and TSTP.
